554	+                run_id=_clean_optional(run_id),
555	+                question_id=_clean_optional(question_id),
556	+                kind=DOCUMENT_BLOB_KIND,
557	+                label=str(label or "document blob").strip() or "document blob",
558	+                content_type=_clean_required(content_type, field_name="content_type"),
559	+                object_key=str(file_location["object_key"]),
560	+                size_bytes=int(size_bytes),
561	+                sha256=str(file_location["sha256"]),
562	+                created_at=utc_now(),
563	+            )
564	+            session.add(row)
565	+        else:
566	+            if str(row.kind) != DOCUMENT_BLOB_KIND:
567	+                raise ValueError(f"artifact_id {normalized_artifact_id!r} is not a document_blob artifact")
568	+            row.job_id = _clean_optional(job_id)
569	+            row.run_id = _clean_optional(run_id)
570	+            row.question_id = _clean_optional(question_id)
571	+            row.label = str(label or row.label or "document blob").strip() or "document blob"
572	+            row.content_type = _clean_required(content_type, field_name="content_type")
573	+            row.object_key = str(file_location["object_key"])
574	+            row.size_bytes = int(size_bytes)
575	+            row.sha256 = str(file_location["sha256"])
576	+        session.flush()
577	+        stored = _artifact_to_document_blob(row, file_location=file_location)
578	+    return stored
579	+
580	+
581	+def attach_document_blob_to_information_node(
582	+    *,
583	+    node_id: str,
584	+    artifact_id: str,
585	+    source_url: str | None = None,
586	+    retrieval_source_id: str | None = None,
587	+    retrieved_at: str | None = None,
588	+    regeneration: dict[str, Any] | None = None,
589	+    fs11_concern_refs: list[dict[str, Any]] | None = None,
590	+    storage_config: StorageRuntimeConfig | None = None,
591	+) -> dict[str, Any]:
592	+    database = ensure_database_schema(storage_config)
593	+    normalized_node_id = _clean_required(node_id, field_name="node_id")
594	+    normalized_artifact_id = _clean_required(artifact_id, field_name="artifact_id")
595	+    with database.session_scope() as session:
596	+        artifact = session.get(ArtifactRow, normalized_artifact_id)
597	+        if artifact is None:
598	+            raise ValueError(f"unknown artifact_id: {normalized_artifact_id!r}")
599	+        if str(artifact.kind) != DOCUMENT_BLOB_KIND:
600	+            raise ValueError(f"artifact_id {normalized_artifact_id!r} is not a document_blob artifact")
601	+        information_node = session.get(InformationNodeRow, normalized_node_id)
602	+        if information_node is None:
603	+            raise ValueError(f"unknown information node_id: {normalized_node_id!r}")
604	+        file_location = build_document_file_location(
605	+            artifact_id=normalized_artifact_id,
606	+            object_key=str(artifact.object_key),
607	+            sha256=str(artifact.sha256),
608	+            content_type=str(artifact.content_type),
609	+            size_bytes=int(artifact.size_bytes),
610	+            source_url=source_url,
611	+            retrieval_source_id=retrieval_source_id,
612	+            retrieved_at=retrieved_at,
613	+            regeneration=regeneration,
614	+            fs11_concern_refs=fs11_concern_refs,
615	+        )
616	+        information_node.file_location = file_location
617	+        information_node.content_type = str(artifact.content_type)
618	+        if source_url:
619	+            information_node.source = str(source_url).strip()
620	+        detail = dict(information_node.detail or {})
621	+        nested_detail = dict(detail.get("detail") or {})
622	+        nested_detail["file_location"] = file_location
623	+        detail["detail"] = nested_detail
624	+        information_node.detail = detail
625	+    return file_location
626	+
627	+
628	+def _node_value(node: Any, key: str) -> str:
629	+    return str(getattr(node, key, "") or "").strip()
630	+
631	+
632	+def _node_data(node: Any) -> dict[str, Any]:
633	+    data = getattr(node, "data", {}) or {}
634	+    return dict(data) if isinstance(data, dict) else {}
635	+
636	+
637	+def _nested_payload(data: dict[str, Any], key: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
638	+    value = data.get(key)
639	+    return dict(value) if isinstance(value, dict) else {}
640	+
641	+
642	+def _node_detail(data: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
643	+    detail = _nested_payload(data, "detail")
644	+    if detail:
645	+        return detail
646	+    return {
647	+        str(key): value
648	+        for key, value in data.items()
649	+        if key not in {"question_id", "question_source", "source_tag", "origin", "provenance"}
650	+    }
651	+
652	+
653	+def _node_provenance(data: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
654	+    return _nested_payload(data, "provenance")
655	+
656	+
657	+def _first_text(*values: Any) -> str | None:
658	+    for value in values:
659	+        text = str(value or "").strip()
660	+        if text:
661	+            return text
662	+    return None
663	+
664	+
665	+def _source_payloads(provenance: dict[str, Any]) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
666	+    return [dict(item) for item in (provenance.get("sources") or []) if isinstance(item, dict)]
667	+
668	+
669	+def _source_url_for_document(*, detail: dict[str, Any], sources: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> str | None:
670	+    candidates: list[Any] = [
671	+        detail.get("source_url"),
672	+        detail.get("pdf_url"),
673	+        detail.get("url"),
674	+    ]
675	+    for source in sources:
676	+        candidates.extend([source.get("source_url"), source.get("pdf_url"), source.get("url")])
677	+    text = _first_text(*candidates)
678	+    if text and text.startswith(("http://", "https://")):
679	+        return text
680	+    return None
681	+
682	+
683	+def _retrieval_source_id(*, detail: dict[str, Any], sources: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> str | None:
684	+    source = sources[0] if sources else {}
685	+    provider = _first_text(source.get("provider"), detail.get("provider")) or "unknown"
686	+    source_ref = _first_text(
687	+        source.get("entry_id"),
688	+        source.get("source_id"),
689	+        source.get("document_number"),
690	+        source.get("source_path"),
691	+        detail.get("document_key"),
692	+        detail.get("filename"),
693	+    )
694	+    if not source_ref:
695	+        return provider
696	+    return f"{provider}:{source_ref}"
697	+
698	+
699	+def _retrieved_at(*, detail: dict[str, Any], sources: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> str:
700	+    source = sources[0] if sources else {}
701	+    return (
702	+        _first_text(
703	+            detail.get("retrieved_at"),
704	+            detail.get("downloaded_at"),
705	+            source.get("retrieved_at"),
706	+            source.get("downloaded_at"),
707	+        )
708	+        or utc_now().isoformat()
709	+    )
710	+
711	+
712	+def _artifact_id_for_document(*, run_id: str, question_id: str, node_id: str, object_key: str, sha256: str) -> str:
713	+    digest = hashlib.sha1(f"{run_id}|{question_id}|{node_id}|{object_key}".encode()).hexdigest()[:16]
714	+    return f"doc_{sha256[:16]}_{digest}"
715	+
716	+
717	+def _regeneration_for_document(
718	+    *,
719	+    artifact_id: str,
720	+    node_id: str,
721	+    detail: dict[str, Any],
722	+    sources: list[dict[str, Any]],
723	+    source_url: str | None,
724	+) -> dict[str, Any]:
725	+    if source_url:
726	+        return {
727	+            "mode": "source_url",
728	+            "source_url": source_url,
729	+            "idempotency_key": source_url,
730	+        }
731	+    source = sources[0] if sources else {}
732	+    provider = _first_text(source.get("provider"), detail.get("provider"))
733	+    stable_ref = _first_text(source.get("entry_id"), source.get("source_id"), source.get("document_number"))
734	+    if provider and stable_ref:
735	+        return {
736	+            "mode": "source_api",
737	+            "source_api": provider,
738	+            "stable_external_ref": stable_ref,
739	+            "artifact_id": artifact_id,
740	+        }
741	+    return {
742	+        "mode": "db_ref",
743	+        "db_ref": f"information_nodes:{node_id}",
744	+        "artifact_id": artifact_id,
745	+        "document_key": _clean_optional(str(detail.get("document_key") or "")),
746	+    }
747	+
748	+
749	+def _write_file_location_back_to_graph_node(node: Any, file_location: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
750	+    data = _node_data(node)
751	+    detail = _node_detail(data)
752	+    detail["file_location"] = dict(file_location)
753	+    data["detail"] = detail
754	+    try:
755	+        node.data = data
756	+    except Exception:
757	+        return
758	+
759	+
760	+def register_fs2_graph_document_blobs(
761	+    *,
762	+    graph: Any,
763	+    run_id: str,
764	+    question_id: str,
765	+    storage_config: StorageRuntimeConfig | None = None,
766	+    strict: bool = False,
767	+) -> GraphDocumentBlobRegistrationResult:
768	+    """Register FS2 evidence-node document bytes as artifact-backed locators.
769	+
770	+    This bridge is intentionally idempotent. It runs after graph persistence so
771	+    `information_nodes` already exist, then updates their `file_location` JSON
772	+    with the artifact-backed shape that FS3 and FS10 can replay.
773	+    """
774	+
775	+    normalized_run_id = _clean_required(run_id, field_name="run_id")
776	+    normalized_question_id = _clean_required(question_id, field_name="question_id")
777	+    registered: list[RegisteredGraphDocumentBlob] = []
778	+    skipped: list[dict[str, str]] = []
779	+
780	+    for node in (getattr(graph, "nodes", {}) or {}).values():
781	+        node_type = _node_value(node, "type").lower()
782	+        if node_type not in {"evidence", "information"}:
783	+            continue
784	+        node_id = _node_value(node, "id") or _node_value(node, "node_id")
785	+        if not node_id:
786	+            continue
787	+        data = _node_data(node)
788	+        if str(data.get("question_id") or "").strip() not in {"", normalized_question_id}:
789	+            continue
790	+        detail = _node_detail(data)
791	+        provenance = _node_provenance(data)
792	+        sources = _source_payloads(provenance)
793	+        object_key = _first_text(detail.get("document_link"), detail.get("local_path"), *(source.get("local_path") for source in sources))
794	+        if not object_key:
795	+            skipped.append({"node_id": node_id, "reason": "missing_document_link"})
796	+            continue
797	+        path = Path(object_key)
798	+        if not path.exists() or not path.is_file():
799	+            skipped.append({"node_id": node_id, "reason": "missing_document_bytes", "object_key": object_key})
800	+            continue
801	+        data_bytes = path.read_bytes()
802	+        sha256 = hashlib.sha256(data_bytes).hexdigest()
803	+        content_type = mimetypes.guess_type(path.name)[0] or "application/octet-stream"
804	+        source_url = _source_url_for_document(detail=detail, sources=sources)
805	+        artifact_id = _artifact_id_for_document(
806	+            run_id=normalized_run_id,
807	+            question_id=normalized_question_id,
808	+            node_id=node_id,
809	+            object_key=object_key,
```

```
Based on my comprehensive review of the design documentation and source code, here is a detailed summary of SPI-232 (Port FS3 extraction, graph admission, and document-pack validation):

---

## COMPREHENSIVE SUMMARY: SPI-232 - FS3 Design & Implementation

### OVERVIEW

FS3 (Supporting entity extraction) is the feature set responsible for turning evidence into causal graph content. It extracts drivers from research evidence, reduces them into manageable sets, retrieves indicator literature, extracts indicators, and prepares graph-ready nodes and conditional edges. The core responsibilities are:

1. **Driver extraction and reduction** — converting evidence chunks into normalized causal drivers
2. **Indicator extraction scoped to drivers** — finding observable signals that predict driver activity
3. **Graph admission boundary enforcement** — validating before graph write
4. **Explanation and provenance records** — documenting how content entered the graph

---

### 1. DRIVER EXTRACTION WITH CANONICAL NODE CREATION & FS3 PROVENANCE

**Current Implementation (from ResearchDriver README & indicator_extraction.py):**

- Driver extraction happens in two places:
  - **Agent01 (retrieval)**: Collects evidence documents scoped to question
  - **Agent02 (extraction/reduction)**: Uses `insight_agent/factor_extraction.py` to extract driver candidates from chunks and reduce them

- **Canonical node creation**:
  - Drivers are extracted as candidates first, then normalized and deduplicated
  - Reduced drivers become the graph-ready set that enters the graph
  - Each driver node carries provenance metadata via `build_provenance()` and `build_origin()` (from `core.node_data`)

- **FS3 provenance tracking** (designed in fs3-supporting-entity-extraction-design.md):
  - Driver candidates grouped by: grounded (from evidence), native (free-knowledge), fallback (template-based)
  - Each reduced driver preserves source-context references linking back to evidence chunks
  - Domain pack system steers which fallback templates apply per domain

**Designed Target State (fs3-supporting-entity-extraction-design.md):**

- Artifact-first validation: emit replayable `driver_extraction.json` with:
  - candidate counts by generation mode (grounded vs fallback vs native)
  - reduced-driver counts and merge decisions
  - source-coverage counters
  - driver-to-target coverage summary
  - reduction diagnostics and dropped-candidate reasons

---

### 2. INDICATOR EXTRACTION SCOPED TO DRIVERS

**Current Implementation (from indicator_extraction.py & indicator_graph_apply.py):**

- **Indicator extraction process**:
  - Per reduced driver, retrieve literature using the driver's search query
  - Claude API extracts candidate indicators from retrieved literature titles
  - Each indicator includes: name, direction (supports/contradicts), relationship_judgment, relationship_description
  - `IndicatorGraphApplicationConfig` controls: max indicators per driver (default 4), edge deduplication, fallback pack

- **Scoping mechanism**:
  - Step 4 of question pipeline uses `driver_indicator_literature.py` to fetch literature scoped to each driver
  - Budget controls from domain pack limit PDFs per driver (`indicator_pdfs_per_driver`)
  - Only indicators for reduced drivers are extracted; not for question as a whole

- **Graph application**:
  - `indicator_graph_apply.py` creates indicator nodes and CONDITIONAL edges linking indicators → drivers
  - Uses `_driver_nodes_index()` to match extracted indicators to their parent drivers
  - Deduplicates edges by normalized label to prevent redundant relationships

**Designed Target State (fs3-fs10-fs11-integration-design.md):**

- Emit replayable `indicator_extraction.json` component artifact with:
  - indicators per driver
  - indicator observability hints (whether observable in live practice)
  - edge direction and confidence fields
  - edge-quality outputs from `conditional_edge_quality.py`
  - per-driver source sufficiency flags

---

### 3. GRAPH ADMISSION BOUNDARY ENFORCEMENT

**Current State & Design:**

The design introduces a new `graph_admission_gate` that runs before graph write and checks:

- **Semantic admissibility** — driver and edge roles are valid for the graph context
- **Schema validity** — graph objects conform to data model contracts
- **Minimum provenance and evidence support** — drivers/indicators have sufficient backing
- **Explanation object presence** — each admitted object has rationale and provenance metadata

**Implementation Plan (fs3-supporting-entity-extraction-design.md):**

Current validation is spread across:
- `insight_agent/graph_contracts.py` (edge contracts)
- `core/node_data.py` (node schema validation)
- `core/schema_validation.py` (data model validation)

Target: Make `graph_admission_gate` explicit and auditable as a named gate before:
```
driver/indicator candidates → reduced set → graph admission gate → write to store
```

**FS10 Component Validation (fs3-fs10-fs11-integration-design.md):**

- FS10 computes verdict for each FS3 output: `ok`, `failed`, or `unsupported`
- Driver extraction `ok` criteria: drivers extracted, schema-valid, provenance counters present
- Indicator extraction `ok` criteria: indicators extracted, schema-valid, edge-quality outputs present

---

### 4. CONDITIONAL & REFERENCE EDGE CREATION

**Current Implementation:**

From `indicator_graph_apply.py`:

- **Edge type**: CONDITIONAL (configurable, default `conditional_edge_weight = 1.0`)
- **Edge creation logic**:
  - Match extracted indicators to driver nodes by normalized title, stripped label, search query
  - Create CONDITIONAL edges from indicator → driver parent
  - Grade edge quality using `conditional_edge_quality.grade_conditional_edge_quality()`

- **Relationship judgment** (from `relationship_judgment.py`):
  - Claude extracts judgment labels: gradient (e.g., "high (for)"), logical (e.g., "B is sufficient for A")
  - Judgment normalized to odds multiplier for edge weighting
  - Used to calibrate strength of indicator→driver causal relationship

- **Reference edges**:
  - Driver nodes carry `source_contexts` list linking to evidence chunks
  - Acts as implicit REFERENCE edges (evidence→driver)
  - Formalized in graph as metadata, not explicit edges (but design allows for explicit REFERENCE edge type)

**Designed Enhancement (promotion-graph-apply-contracts.md):**

- Driver promotion tool proposes decomposing drivers into sub-questions
- Adds capability for PROMOTES edges (driver → sub-question target)
- Controlled synthetic document packs separate from live Brier/outcome truth (see next section)

---

### 5. CONTROLLED SYNTHETIC DOCUMENT PACKS

**Current System (from ResearchDriver README & fs3-supporting-entity-extraction-design.md):**

- **Domain packs** (`services/worker/agents/ResearchDriver/domain_packs/`):
  - Each pack has fallback templates for drivers and indicators
  - Templates enable free-knowledge fallback when retrieval is insufficient
  - Pack routing selects domain-appropriate pack per question (generic_arxiv is fallback)

- **Fallback behavior**:
  - Agent02 tries LLM free-knowledge generation first
  - If empty/error, calls pack's `build_free_knowledge_fallback(...)` with template substitution
  - Fallback rows tagged with metadata for analyst review
  - Fallback policy recorded in `drivers.json` and `sources.json`

**Controlled Separation (designed for FS3-FS10-FS11):**

- Candidates split by generation mode: grounded (evidence-backed), native (LLM free-knowledge), fallback (template)
- FS10 tracks `grounded-driver share on replay set ≥ 60%` (Placeholder threshold)
- FS11 QC axis `fallback_discipline` scores how much output relies on templates vs evidence
- Fallback count reported in `driver_extraction.json` diagnostics

**Document packs NOT mixed with outcome truth:**

- FS10 validation uses frozen Polymarket markets as ground truth (resolved questions)
- Synthetic documents from domain packs are **training/fallback only**, never used as evaluation labels
- Training data transform (Section 3 of fs10-validation-and-calibration-design.md) explicitly separates:
  - `role_training` artifacts from resolved Polymarket questions (training data)
  - `component_validation` artifacts from FS3 extraction (replayable but not directly training-eligible)

---

### 6. GATE REQUIREMENTS: P-FS3-01, A-FS3-01, VD-GRAPH-CONSTRUCTION

The design documents reference placeholder gate thresholds but don't define these specific gate IDs in the files provided. However, the analogous design specifies these gate concepts:

**From fs3-fs10-fs11-sequencing-plan.md (Done-gate table):**

| Metric | Threshold | Evidence Tag |
|--------|-----------|--------------|
| FS3 artifact completeness | 100% of FS3 stages emit valid component artifacts | Placeholder |
| Reduction reproducibility on frozen fixtures | ≥ 99% | Placeholder |
| Grounded-driver share on replay set | ≥ 60% | Placeholder |
| Indicator observability coverage | ≥ 80% | Placeholder |
| FS11 hard-fail precision on reviewed sample | ≥ 80% (Assumed) | Assumed |
| Existing FS3 pipeline regression budget | 0 failing current FS3 contract tests | Measured |

**Likely mapping:**
- **P-FS3-01** (Preservation): FS3 outputs preserve both grounded and fallback provenance + artifact completeness
- **A-FS3-01** (Admissibility): Graph admission gate validates semantic admissibility, schema, provenance, and explanation objects
- **VD-GRAPH-CONSTRUCTION**: Driver-to-target and indicator-to-driver graph construction follows schema and produces observable edges

---

### 7. DRIVER PROMOTION TOOL / PROMOTION-GRAPH-APPLY CONTRACTS

**Purpose** (from driver_promotion_tool.py):

Given a driver's context and its parent question, propose structured MECE (mutually exclusive, collectively exhaustive) outcome options suitable for a standalone sub-question.

**Contracts:**

```python
Input:
  - driver_label: string
  - driver_description: string
  - driver_mechanism: string
  - parent_question_label, options, classification, domain, closing_date
  - edge_judgments: list of relationship judgments

Output (DriverPromotionProposalTool response):
  - question_label: proposed question text
  - classification: binary | categorical | numeric_bracket
  - options: list[{label, description, suggested_baseline_p}]
  - rationale: why this decomposition makes sense
```

**Graph application contract:**

When a driver is promoted to a sub-question:
- Creates a new target node (sub-question)
- Driver node becomes a PROMOTES edge source → sub-question target
- Sub-question inherits question metadata (domain, closing date, etc.)
- Sub-question question-set separate from parent question to maintain leakage safety

**Design status:** Specified in `driver_promotion_tool.py`; graph-apply contract deferred to Phase D of FS3-FS10-FS11 sequencing.

---

### 8. CROSS-REFERENCES & CANONICAL SOURCES

**Primary Design Documents:**

1. **fs3-supporting-entity-extraction-design.md** — base FS3 spec defining scope, canonical terms, core behavior, data structures
2. **fs3-fs10-fs11-integration-design.md** — integration of FS3 with validation (FS10) and QC (FS11)
3. **fs3-fs10-fs11-sequencing-plan.md** — four phases: (A) canonical artifacts, (B) FS10 component validation, (C) FS11 QC, (D) workflow gating
4. **fs3-fs10-fs11-test-plan.md** — unit/component/integration/end-to-end test matrix and fixtures
5. **fs3-fs10-fs11-design-review.md** — critical analysis: what's working, readability issues, logical inconsistencies, evidence gaps, rollout risks
6. **fs1-fs3-fs10-fs11-shared-contracts.md** — shared upstream concern shape, component artifact envelope, QC scorecard, QC index extension, metric evidence tags

**Implementation Source:**

- `core/research_driver/indicator_extraction.py` — indicator extraction via Claude API
- `core/research_driver/indicator_graph_apply.py` — conditional-edge creation and indicator node application
- `core/research_driver/driver_promotion_tool.py` — driver promotion decomposition
- `services/worker/agents/ResearchDriver/README.md` — architecture, domain packs, orchestration
- `insight_agent/factor_extraction.py` — driver candidate extraction and reduction (mentioned but not fully read)

**FS10 Context (fs10-validation-and-calibration-design.md):**

Defines component validation role, training data transformation, and track records that FS3 feeds into:
- FS3 emits `driver_extraction.json` and `indicator_extraction.json` as `component_validation` artifacts
- These are replayable, not directly training-eligible unless explicit transform promotes them
- FS10 tracks `source_question_id` and `split_group_id` across target/driver/indicator roles from resolved questions

---

### 9. KEY DESIGN PRINCIPLES

**From design docs:**

1. **Artifact-first validation** — inspection before gating; raw upstream artifacts are annotated, never replaced by QC outputs
2. **Separation of concerns** — FS3 owns extraction/admission; FS10 owns measurement; FS11 owns judgment
3. **Replayability** — all FS3 outputs must be reproducible without live LLM calls (via frozen fixtures)
4. **Grounding discipline** — fallback counts tracked; QC axis penalizes over-reliance on templates
5. **Leakage safety** — train/eval splits enforced across target/driver/indicator roles; single split per question_id
6. **Provenance transparency** — every admitted driver/indicator/edge carries explanation object with evidence basis, rationale, originating subsystem, semantic type

---

### 10. OPEN QUESTIONS & RISKS (from design review)

**From fs3-fs10-fs11-design-review.md:**

- **Readability**: FS3 currently spread across multiple files with no canonical architecture doc (this design aims to fix that)
- **Logical inconsistencies**:
  - Fallback-heavy outputs visible but no canonical replay metric that owns them
  - Edge grading exists but not clearly integrated with FS11 scorecard
  - Upstream_fs="FS3" hints can predate canonical FS3 QC artifact
- **Evidence gaps**:
  - No checked-in benchmark for "good driver set" or "good indicator set"
  - Proposed thresholds (60% grounded, 80% observable) are provisional and domain-varying
  - Relationship between FS3 replay metrics and final forecast accuracy partly proxy-based
- **Rollout risks**:
  - Replay metrics could bias toward bland, generic, high-consensus drivers
  - Deterministic scorecard could penalize uncommon but correct causal structures
  - Blocking on FS3 QC too early could slow analyst workflow without enough validation evidence

**Recommended mitigations:**
- Keep driver and indicator artifacts separate from start
- Preserve raw candidate artifacts even when reduced outputs score better
- Calibrate FS11 against reviewed samples before enabling blocking behavior
- Treat fallback ratio as diagnostic, not as the only quality measure

---

### DELIVERABLES THIS DESIGN DEFINES

**Phase A (Canonical FS3 component artifacts):**
- Replayable `driver_extraction.json` with grounded/fallback/native counts, reduction diagnostics
- Replayable `indicator_extraction.json` with observability hints, edge quality, per-driver sufficiency
- QC index slots for FS3 scorecards and concerns

**Phase B (FS10 component validation):**
- FS10 runner support for FS3 artifacts
- Metrics: grounding share, target coverage, reduction stability, indicator observability
- Replay stratification by question type and domain
- Split-group and leakage-status propagation

**Phase C (FS11 FS3 QC):**
- Deterministic scorecards for drivers and indicators
- Axes: grounding, target_coverage, redundancy_control, causal_specificity, indicator_observability, edge_plausibility, fallback_discipline, downstream_compatibility
- Structured FS3 concerns routing to analysts

**Phase D (Workflow gating & feedback loops):**
- Analyst review surfaces for weak/hard-fail FS3 outputs
- Optional gating for severe QC failures
- Feedback path from replay/QC failures into prompt/reduction/fallback-policy tuning

---

This completes the comprehensive research summary for SPI-232. The design provides a clear path from current FS3 scatter to canonical, measurable, auditable driver extraction and admission with full provenance tracking and QC integration.

### Summaries of Existing Files

**1. `core/research_driver/driver_promotion_tool.py`**
LLM tool for proposing sub-question targets when promoting a driver. Key symbols: `DriverPromotionProposalTool` (a `BaseLLMTool` subclass), `propose_driver_promotion()` (convenience function). Uses the prompt at `FS8/driver_promotion/system.md`. Returns structured JSON with `question_label`, `classification`, `options`, `rationale`. Falls back to a binary Yes/No proposal if the LLM fails. Loaded via `resolve_role_model("tool")`.

**3. `services/api/routes/driver_promotion.py`**
Two FastAPI endpoints:
- `POST /questions/{qid}/drivers/{did}/propose-promotion` -- calls the LLM tool, rate-limited 5/min, read-only.
- `POST /questions/{qid}/drivers/{did}/promote` -- executes promotion: removes the original driver, creates replacement driver nodes (one per confirmed option), rewires CONDITIONAL and REFERENCE edges, creates a new sub-question via `reconcile_question_core_graph_bundle`, sets bidirectional `companion_node_id` links between replacement drivers and sub-question targets, marks parent question workflow steps stale, persists everything, and optionally triggers a research pipeline job. Helper `_resolve_graph_id` finds the graph containing the question's nodes.

**7. `tests/test_driver_promotion.py`**
Tests for SPI-67 driver promotion feature. Covers:
- LLM proposal returns structured output
- Binary fallback on LLM error or empty options
- Pydantic contract validation (`DriverPromotionExecuteRequest`, `DriverPromotionProposal`, `DriverPromotionResult`)
- Companion node posterior sync (flow-back from sub-question targets to companion drivers)
- Bidirectional `companion_node_id` convention via `ensure_question_target_structure`

**8. `tests/test_insight_agent_graph_apply.py`**
Tests for `graph_apply` module (insight agent driver application). Key tests:
- `apply_reduced_drivers_to_graph` creates driver nodes with correct data (origin, provenance, detail fields including `effect_profile`, `retrieval_scopes`, `tier_rank`, `grounding_status`, `compensation_mode`) and CONDITIONAL/REFERENCE edges with per-target relationship judgments and odds multipliers
- Source context refs used for REFERENCE edges when `pdf_refs` is empty
- `_add_conditional_edges` skips flat profiles for binary targets (drops driver)
- Low-confidence fallback edges for unresolved target assessments
- Edge quality grading resilience (keeps edges when grader tool fails, falls back to minimum scores)
- Orphan driver rollback (SPI-200): drivers are dropped when target assessments are unresolved or flat, including cleanup of REFERENCE edges

**9. `tests/test_insight_agent_graph_contracts.py`**
Single test: `test_add_driver_node_from_factor_sets_ontology_version`. Verifies that `_add_driver_node_from_factor` sets `ontology_version` on the driver node's detail, using `ONTOLOGY_OBJECT_SPEC_VERSION_V2` from `core.research_driver.driver_ontology`.

**10. `core/research_driver/driver_indicator_literature.py`**
Runs Agent01 (literature evidence retrieval) per reduced driver to fetch indicator-supporting PDFs. Key symbols: `DriverIndicatorLiteratureConfig`, `run_driver_indicator_literature_for_question()`, `prioritize_drivers_for_indicator_literature()`, `driver_scope_to_agent_scope()`, `build_driver_indicator_query()`. Uses `ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=3)` for parallel retrieval. Writes per-driver `result.json` files. Maps driver scopes to agent scopes (company_specific, industry_specific, market_wide, cross_cutting_shock).

**11. `core/research_driver/indicator_extraction.py`**
Indicator extraction from driver literature using Claude API via LiteLLM. Key symbols: `DriverIndicatorExtractionTool`, `extract_indicator_candidates_for_driver_payload()`, `batch_extract_indicator_candidates()`, `prepare_extraction_prompt()`, `validate_extraction_response()`, `normalize_indicator_rows()`. Uses prompt `FS3/indicator_extraction/system.md`. Extracts market-observable indicators with direction (supports/contradicts), relationship_judgment (gradient or logical labels from the allowed set), relationship_description, rationale, and confidence. Has a multi-layer fallback: LLM -> pack-specific fallback -> empty. Marks no-source indicators as `llm_best_effort_no_sources`. Batch mode is sequential (not using Anthropic Batch API due to polling overhead).

**12. `core/research_driver/indicator_graph_apply.py`**
Creates indicator nodes and CONDITIONAL edges from extraction results. Key symbols: `IndicatorGraphApplicationConfig`, `apply_indicators_from_driver_literature()`. Matches driver rows to graph nodes by normalized title or search query. Upserts indicator nodes (dedupes by normalized label). Creates indicator-to-driver CONDITIONAL edges with relationship judgment, odds multiplier, edge quality grading, and source metadata. Computes a composite `stage_outcome` with status (ok/partial/failed) and quality flags. Tracks detailed per-driver summaries including fallback usage and review-required flags.

**14. `tests/test_indicator_graph_apply.py`**
Comprehensive test suite (~45KB) covering:
- Generic pack returns no synthetic fallback
- Commodities pack returns pack-specific indicators
- Best-effort LLM indicators when no sources available
- Prompt preparation and validation
- Full graph application: indicator nodes + CONDITIONAL edges created with correct relationship judgments, odds multipliers, edge quality
- Edge deduplication on repeat runs
- Driver matching by prefix and search query fallback
- Missing driver rows handling
- Source metadata merging
- Stage outcome computation for ok/partial/failed scenarios
- Batch extraction (sequential mode)

## SPI-232 File-by-File Research Summary

### OWNED FILES (Core Deliverables)

---

#### 1. `core/research_driver/driver_promotion_tool.py`

**Summary:** LLM tool for proposing sub-question targets when promoting a driver to a standalone question. Uses Claude to generate MECE outcome options.

**Key classes/functions:**
- `DriverPromotionProposalTool(BaseLLMTool)` -- LLM tool that builds a prompt from driver context and parent question metadata, returns structured JSON with question_label, classification, options, rationale
- `propose_driver_promotion(*, driver_label, driver_description, driver_mechanism, parent_question_label, parent_options, parent_classification, parent_domain, parent_closing_date, edge_judgments, model) -> dict` -- Public entry point. Calls the LLM tool with fallback to binary Yes/No if the LLM fails or returns empty options.

**FS3 relevance:** This is FS8 (driver promotion) functionality but intersects with FS3 because promoted drivers produce replacement driver nodes and new sub-question graphs that go through the same extraction pipeline.

**Dependencies:** `packages.inference.llm.BaseLLMTool`, `packages.inference.llm.LLMConfig`

---

#### 2. `fs10_validation/components/fs3_extraction.py`

**Summary:** Builds FS3 extraction artifacts for FS10 validation runs. Produces `ExtractionArtifact` records that describe driver and indicator nodes with their grounding/fallback/quality status.

**Key classes/functions:**
- `ExtractionNodeRecord` (frozen dataclass) -- Per-node record with: node_id, node_label, role (driver/indicator), source_mode, generation_mode, grounding_status, epistemic_status, source_context_count, review_required, quality_flags, concern_patterns
- `ExtractionArtifact` (frozen dataclass) -- Collection of records for a question_id+role, with computed properties: grounded_count, fallback_count, review_required_count
- `build_driver_extraction_artifact(*, nodes, question_id) -> ExtractionArtifact`
- `build_indicator_extraction_artifact(*, nodes, question_id) -> ExtractionArtifact`
- `_is_grounded(record)` -- True if source_context_count > 0 or grounding_status not in {"", "ungrounded", "llm_compensated"}
- `_uses_fallback(record)` -- True if source_mode or generation_mode contains "fallback"/"free_knowledge", or grounding_status == "llm_compensated"

**FS3 relevance:** This is the core FS3 validation component -- it inspects each node's data.detail for source_mode, generation_mode, grounding_status, epistemic_status, quality_flags, and concern patterns to assess extraction quality.

**Dependencies:** `core.node_data.node_detail`

---

#### 3. `services/api/routes/driver_promotion.py`

**Summary:** FastAPI endpoints for driver-to-question promotion (two phases: propose, then execute). The largest owned file (~350 lines).

**Key endpoints:**
- `POST /questions/{qid}/drivers/{did}/propose-promotion` -- Read-only; validates driver ownership, collects parent context and edge judgments, calls `propose_driver_promotion`, returns `DriverPromotionProposal`
- `POST /questions/{qid}/drivers/{did}/promote` -- Executes promotion: removes original driver, creates replacement driver nodes (one per option), rewires CONDITIONAL and REFERENCE edges, creates a new sub-question with `QuestionRecord`, scaffolds the sub-question graph via `reconcile_question_core_graph_bundle`, sets bidirectional companion_node_id links between replacement drivers and sub-question targets, marks parent workflow steps stale, saves everything, optionally auto-submits a research job

**Key helpers:**
- `_generate_sub_question_id(questions, parent_qid) -> str` -- Generates `{parent_qid}_sq_{N}` IDs
- `_resolve_graph_id(...)` -- Finds the graph_id containing nodes for the question

**FS3 relevance:** The promote endpoint creates new sub-question graphs that must pass FS3 extraction and graph admission. The new QuestionRecord carries `hierarchy_role: "sub_question"`, `driver_context` with the original driver's metadata, and `question_source: "driver_promotion"`.

**Dependencies:** `core.research_driver.data_model.BayesianGraph`, `core.research_driver.question_core_graph.reconcile_question_core_graph_bundle`, `core.research_driver.question_workflow`, `core.research_driver.document_review`, `core.research_driver.relationship_judgment`, `packages.contracts.questions.*`, `packages.storage.*`

---

#### 4. `tests/test_fs3_qc.py`

**Summary:** Tests the DB-backed FS3 quality-control (QC) pipeline: persisting driver/indicator scorecards and concern records, then verifying gate decisions and review overrides.

**Key tests:**
- `test_fs3_qc_persists_driver_and_indicator_concerns_via_db_authority` -- Creates a graph with a weak driver (sparse_driver_grounding) and weak indicator (thin_indicator_support), persists via `persist_graph_scorecards`, then verifies 2 scorecards, 2 concerns, gate_status="review_required", review_state="pending"
- `test_fs3_qc_review_override_round_trips_without_mutating_raw_outputs` -- Tests `update_question_gate_review` for analyst override to "approved"/"pass" without mutating underlying concern data

**Dependencies:** `packages.domain.qc` (list_question_scorecards, load_question_concerns, load_question_gate_decision, persist_graph_scorecards, update_question_gate_review)

---

#### 5. `tests/test_fs3_upstream_concerns.py`

**Summary:** Tests that upstream FS3 concerns (e.g., fallback_overuse) preserve node-level traceability in DB rows.

**Key test:**
- `test_fs3_upstream_concerns_keep_node_level_traceability_in_db_rows` -- A driver with `estimation_concerns` tagged upstream_fs="FS3" is persisted; verifies the concern row retains node_id, upstream_fs, detected_pattern, and full details dict.

**Dependencies:** `packages.domain.qc` (load_question_concerns, persist_graph_scorecards)

---

#### 6. `tests/test_fs3_validation_replay.py`

**Summary:** Integration test for the FS10 validation runner with FS3 extraction artifacts.

**Key test:**
- `test_fs3_validation_runner_persists_db_backed_driver_and_indicator_component_artifacts` -- Sets up a full nodes.json with one grounded driver and one fallback indicator, runs `run_fs10_validation`, then checks that FS3 component artifacts are persisted with correct grounded_count, fallback_count, review_required_count. Also verifies artifacts survive file deletion (DB-backed authority).

**Dependencies:** `fs10_validation.catalog.HistoricQuestion`, `fs10_validation.component_store.list_component_artifacts_for_run`, `fs10_validation.runner.run_fs10_validation`

---

#### 7. `tests/test_driver_promotion.py`

**Summary:** Comprehensive test suite for driver promotion (SPI-67). Tests the LLM tool, Pydantic contracts, companion node sync, and the sub-question target structure.

**Key tests:**
- `test_propose_driver_promotion_returns_structured_proposal` -- Mocks LLM success
- `test_propose_driver_promotion_falls_back_to_binary_on_error` -- Verifies binary fallback on LLM failure
- `test_propose_driver_promotion_falls_back_when_empty_options` -- Empty options triggers fallback
- `test_driver_promotion_execute_request_validates_options` -- Pydantic contract test
- `test_sync_posteriors_to_companion_drivers` -- Verifies posterior flow-back from sub-question targets to companion drivers via `_sync_posteriors_to_companion_drivers`
- `test_companion_node_id_set_on_both_sides` -- Verifies `StoredQuestionGraph.ensure_question_target_structure` creates target nodes that can be paired with companion drivers

**Dependencies:** `core.research_driver.driver_promotion_tool`, `packages.contracts.questions`, `packages.storage.graph_store`, `services.worker.research_driver_question_pipeline._sync_posteriors_to_companion_drivers`, `core.research_driver.question_core_graph`

---

#### 8. `tests/test_insight_agent_graph_apply.py`

**Summary:** Regression tests for driver graph application (how reduced drivers become graph nodes with CONDITIONAL edges to targets).

**Key tests:**
- `test_apply_reduced_drivers_adds_conditional_edges_to_targets` -- Full contract test: verifies driver node data (origin, provenance, detail with summary/mechanism/effect_profile/retrieval_scopes/tier_rank/grounding_status), REFERENCE edge from evidence, 3 CONDITIONAL edges with per-target relationship_judgments and odds_multipliers, edge_quality dict
- `test_apply_reduced_drivers_uses_source_context_refs_for_reference_edges` -- Tests LLM-compensated drivers with source_contexts for evidence linking
- `test_add_conditional_edges_skips_flat_profiles_for_binary_targets` -- Flat judgment profiles (same judgment on all targets) produce zero edges
- `test_add_conditional_edges_adds_low_confidence_fallback_when_unresolved` -- Unresolved target assessments get low-confidence fallback edges
- `test_add_conditional_edges_keeps_edges_when_edge_grading_tool_raises` -- Edge grading failures don't block edge creation
- Orphan driver tests (SPI-200): `test_orphan_driver_dropped_when_target_assessments_unresolved`, `test_orphan_driver_dropped_when_flat_judgment_profile`, `test_driver_kept_when_conditional_edges_created_successfully`

**Dependencies:** `core.research_driver.data_model.BayesianGraph`, `services.worker.agents.ResearchDriver.insight_agent.graph_apply` (apply_reduced_drivers_to_graph, _add_conditional_edges, DriverGraphApplicationConfig)

---

#### 9. `tests/test_insight_agent_graph_contracts.py`

**Summary:** Small contract test for `_add_driver_node_from_factor`.

**Key test:**
- `test_add_driver_node_from_factor_sets_ontology_version` -- Verifies that the ontology_version field is set to `ONTOLOGY_OBJECT_SPEC_VERSION_V2` when creating a driver node from a factor dict.

**Dependencies:** `core.research_driver.driver_ontology.ONTOLOGY_OBJECT_SPEC_VERSION_V2`, `services.worker.agents.ResearchDriver.insight_agent.graph_contracts._add_driver_node_from_factor`

---

### SHARED FILES (FS3-specific parts)

---

#### 10. `core/research_driver/driver_indicator_literature.py`

**Summary:** Orchestrates indicator literature retrieval for drivers. Prioritizes drivers, maps scopes to agent-compatible values, and builds retrieval queries. (~509 lines)

**Key functions:**
- `prioritize_drivers_for_indicator_literature(reduced_drivers) -> list[dict]` -- Ranks drivers by evidence_refs > 0, not-fallback, support_level, original order
- `driver_scope_to_agent_scope(driver) -> str` -- Maps scope strings to one of: company_specific, industry_specific, market_wide, cross_cutting_shock
- `build_driver_indicator_query(driver) -> str` -- Builds a retrieval query from driver metadata + indicator-specific terms
- `_safe_driver_slug(title, index) -> str` -- Sanitized slug for file/folder naming
- `_selected_sources_from_driver_refs(driver) -> list[dict]` -- Carries grounded driver evidence titles into indicator extraction

**FS3 relevance:** This is the pipeline step that connects FS3 driver extraction to indicator literature retrieval. It determines which drivers get indicator retrieval and what queries are used.

**Dependencies:** `core.research_driver.data_model.BayesianGraph`, `core.research_driver.fallback_driver.is_fallback_driver`, `services.worker.agents.ResearchDriver.research_agent.agent`, `packages.inference.llm.concurrency`, `packages.storage`

---

#### 11. `core/research_driver/indicator_extraction.py`

**Summary:** LLM-based indicator extraction from driver literature. Extracts market-observable indicators that causally predict driver activation. (~370 lines)

**Key classes/functions:**
- `DriverIndicatorExtractionTool` -- Wrapper class with `run(**kwargs)` method using LLMRuntime for structured output
- `prepare_extraction_prompt(*, driver_title, driver_query, source_titles, max_indicators) -> (system_prompt, user_prompt)` -- Builds prompts with explicit instructions for relationship_judgment labels (gradient + logical)
- `extract_causal_relationships(*, driver_title, driver_query, source_titles, max_indicators) -> dict` -- Direct LiteLLM API call path
- `validate_extraction_response(raw) -> list[dict]` -- Pydantic validation of LLM output
- `normalize_indicator_rows(*, rows_in, driver_title) -> list[dict]` -- Normalizes direction, relationship_judgment, confidence
- `extract_indicator_candidates_for_driver_payload(*, driver_title, driver_query, source_titles, max_indicators, llm_enabled, fallback_pack_name, precomputed_llm_result) -> dict` -- Main public API. Returns dict with indicators, pack_name, fallback_used, review_required, stage_outcome
- `batch_extract_indicator_candidates(drivers, *, max_indicators, llm_enabled, fallback_pack_name) -> list[dict]` -- Batch extraction (currently sequential)

**FS3 relevance:** This is the core FS3 indicator extraction module. It produces indicator candidates with relationship_judgment labels, confidence scores, source_mode tags (grounded vs fallback vs best-effort), and epistemic_status markers that feed into graph admission.

**Dependencies:** `packages.inference.llm`, `core.text_utils`, `packages.domain.research_driver.stage_outcomes`, `services.worker.agents.ResearchDriver.research_agent.retrieval_packs`, `core.research_driver.relationship_judgment`

---

#### 12. `core/research_driver/indicator_graph_apply.py`

**Summary:** Creates indicator nodes and CONDITIONAL edges from extraction results, linking indicators to parent drivers. (~811 lines)

**Key classes/functions:**
- `IndicatorGraphApplicationConfig` (frozen dataclass) -- Config with conditional_edge_type, reference_edge_type, max_indicators_per_driver, llm_enabled, dedupe_edges, fallback_pack_name
- `apply_indicators_from_driver_literature(graph, *, question_id, indicator_literature_payload, toi, cfg) -> dict` -- Main entry point. Matches driver rows to graph nodes, batch-extracts indicators, creates/updates indicator nodes with proper origin/provenance/detail, creates CONDITIONAL edges (indicator->driver) and REFERENCE edges (information->indicator), computes aggregate stage_outcome
- `_upsert_indicator_node(graph, *, ind, indicator_lookup, ...) -> (indicator_id, was_created)` -- Creates or updates indicator nodes, stripping relation detail from node data (rationale/confidence go on edges only)
- `_create_indicator_edge(graph, *, ind, indicator_id, driver_id, ...) -> None` -- Creates CONDITIONAL edge with normalized relationship_judgment, odds_multiplier, edge_quality grading
- `_create_indicator_reference_edges(graph, *, ind, indicator_id, selected_sources, ...) -> (created, skipped)` -- Creates REFERENCE edges from information nodes to indicators (skips inferred indicators)
- `_collect_eligible_drivers(rows, ...) -> (eligible_drivers, eligible_meta)` -- Matches literature rows to graph driver nodes by title, search_query, or ID
- `_compute_indicator_stage_outcome(*, per_driver_stage_statuses, stats) -> dict` -- Computes aggregate status: ok, partial (mixed quality, missing driver nodes, review-required), or failed

**FS3 relevance:** This is the second half of FS3 -- after extraction, this module materializes indicators into the graph. It enforces the graph entity structure (indicator->driver CONDITIONAL, information->indicator REFERENCE), handles deduplication, and tracks quality metrics.

**Dependencies:** `core.node_data`, `core.research_driver.document_review`, `core.research_driver.conditional_edge_quality`, `core.research_driver.indicator_extraction`, `core.research_driver.relationship_judgment`, `packages.domain.research_driver.stage_outcomes`

---

#### 13. `fs10_validation/graph_construction.py`

**Summary:** Builds controlled question-plus-document fixture packages (VD-GRAPH-CONSTRUCTION) for local FS3 tuning and validation. DB-backed, not file-backed. (~400 lines)

**Key classes/functions:**
- `build_graph_construction_package(*, records, package_id, min_count, freeze, approved_by, storage_config) -> GraphConstructionBuildResult` -- Builds a validation package from fixture records. Each record has: fixture_id, question_id, question_text, documents, gold_drivers, gold_indicators, distractors, near_misses, graph_admission. Validates inputs, creates source/adapter/candidate/package/rubric/threshold/label/split/leakage/exclusion records in DB. Default min_count=1000.
- `persist_graph_construction_tune_evidence(*, package_id, candidate_id, split_name, question_results, storage_config) -> GraphConstructionTuneEvidenceResult` -- Persists local FS3 tune evidence from a frozen package. Records validation run context plus FS3 component artifacts (driver_extraction, indicator_extraction).

**FS3 relevance:** This is the validation data authority for FS3 graph construction. The fixture records define gold_drivers, gold_indicators, and graph_admission expectations that serve as ground truth for evaluating FS3 extraction quality. Gate IDs: P-FS3-01, A-FS3-01.

**Dependencies:** `fs10_validation.component_store`, `fs10_validation.real_tune_evaluator`, `fs10_validation.run_store`, `fs10_validation.scoring`, `fs10_validation.validation_data_store`, `packages.storage.database`, `packages.storage.runtime_config`

---

#### 14. `tests/test_indicator_graph_apply.py`

**Summary:** Comprehensive regression/contract tests for indicator graph application. (~600+ lines)

**Key tests:**
- `test_indicator_extraction_generic_pack_returns_no_synthetic_fallback` -- generic_arxiv pack returns no fallback indicators
- `test_indicator_extraction_commodities_pack_returns_pack_specific_indicators` -- Commodities pack returns domain-specific fallback indicators with correct source_mode, relationship_judgment
- `test_indicator_extraction_without_sources_uses_best_effort_llm` -- No-source extraction marks indicators as llm_best_effort_no_sources / hypothesis
- `test_apply_indicators_creates_indicator_nodes_and_conditional_edges` -- Full contract: verifies indicator origin (producer=indicator_literature, stage=indicator_fallback), provenance, detail (source_mode, epistemic_status), CONDITIONAL edges with relationship_judgment and odds_multiplier
- `test_apply_indicators_links_source_backed_information_to_indicator` -- REFERENCE edge from information node to indicator when source is matched
- `test_apply_indicators_dedupes_edges_on_repeat_runs` -- Idempotency via edge deduplication
- `test_apply_indicators_strips_relation_detail_from_existing_indicator_nodes` -- rationale/confidence stripped from node data on upsert
- `test_apply_indicators_matches_driver_prefix_and_search_query_fallback` -- Driver matching by "Driver: " prefix stripping and search_query fallback
- `test_apply_indicators_keeps_edges_when_edge_grading_tool_raises` -- Edge quality grading failure produces edge_quality.status="llm_error"

**Dependencies:** Same as file 12 above.

---

#### 15. `tests/test_graph_entity_validation.py`

**Summary:** Tests for the graph entity validation rules that enforce canonical graph structure.

**Key tests:**
- `test_graph_entity_validator_accepts_canonical_endpoint_pairs` -- Valid graph: information->indicator REFERENCE, information->driver REFERENCE, indicator->driver CONDITIONAL, driver->target CONDITIONAL
- `test_graph_entity_validator_flags_information_target_reference_edges` -- information->target REFERENCE is invalid
- `test_graph_entity_validator_flags_qnode_driver_and_driver_target_reference` -- qnode_as_driver + driver->target REFERENCE flagged
- `test_graph_entity_validator_flags_question_nodes_and_duplicate_targets` -- persisted_question_node + duplicate_target_option
- `test_graph_entity_validator_flags_shared_indicator_multiple_drivers` -- Indicators must be scoped to one driver
- `test_full_graph_accepts_complete_driver_target_fanout` -- Full graph: every driver must have edges to every target
- `test_full_graph_rejects_incomplete_driver_target_fanout` -- Missing driver->target edges flagged in "full" mode
- `test_driver_indicator_graph_accepts_target_free_information_indicator_driver_slice` -- "driver_indicator" graph_kind accepts no targets

---

#### 16. `packages/domain/graph_entity_validation.py`

**Summary:** Validation helpers that enforce canonical graph entity definitions. (~280 lines)

**Key types/constants:**
- `CANONICAL_NODE_TYPES = {"target", "driver", "indicator", "information"}`
- `LEGACY_NODE_TYPE_ALIASES = {"option": "target", "evidence": "information"}`
- `VALID_CONDITIONAL_ENDPOINTS = {(indicator,driver), (indicator,target), (driver,driver), (driver,target)}`
- `VALID_REFERENCE_ENDPOINTS = {(information,indicator), (information,driver)}`
- `VALID_REFERENCE_KINDS` -- source/evidence for info->indicator, info->driver
- `VALID_GRAPH_KINDS = {"generic", "driver_indicator", "full"}`
- `GraphEntityValidationIssue` (frozen dataclass) -- code, message, subject_id, details

**Key function:**
- `validate_graph_entities(*, nodes, edges, question_ids, graph_kind) -> list[GraphEntityValidationIssue]` -- Validates: no persisted question nodes, no qnode-as-driver, no unknown types, no duplicate target options, correct CONDITIONAL/REFERENCE endpoint pairs, valid reference_kinds, shared indicator detection, and (in "full" mode) complete driver->target fanout

**FS3 relevance:** This is the graph admission gate. It defines which node/edge combinations are structurally valid. FS3 extraction outputs (drivers, indicators, information nodes, edges) must pass these rules to be admitted to the persisted graph.

**Dependencies:** `packages.storage.EdgeRecord`, `packages.storage.NodeRecord`

---

### Cross-File Dependency Map (FS3 Flow)

1. **Driver extraction** (via insight_agent `graph_apply` and `graph_contracts`) produces driver nodes with origin, provenance, detail, and CONDITIONAL edges to targets
2. **Indicator literature retrieval** (`driver_indicator_literature.py`) prioritizes drivers and retrieves literature
3. **Indicator extraction** (`indicator_extraction.py`) calls LLM to extract indicator candidates from literature
4. **Indicator graph application** (`indicator_graph_apply.py`) materializes indicators as nodes with CONDITIONAL edges to drivers and REFERENCE edges from information nodes
5. **Graph entity validation** (`graph_entity_validation.py`) validates the resulting graph structure against canonical entity definitions
6. **FS3 extraction artifacts** (`fs3_extraction.py`) summarize the quality of the extraction (grounded vs fallback vs review-required counts)
7. **FS3 QC** (`packages/domain/qc`) persists scorecards, concerns, and gate decisions for the extraction
8. **Graph construction validation** (`graph_construction.py`) provides controlled fixture packages with gold_drivers/gold_indicators/graph_admission expectations for evaluating FS3 quality
9. **Driver promotion** (`driver_promotion_tool.py`, `driver_promotion.py`) creates sub-questions from promoted drivers, feeding back into the same extraction pipeline

"""FS11 — Analytical Quality Control.

Per FS11 design doc: this package provides defensibility scoring for
`BaselineEstimate` outputs from FS4. It does NOT own selection — FS4's
mechanical selector reads the scores and picks (highest wins; ties merge
per FS4 §17.7).

Phase B exposed a deterministic stub `DefensibilityJudge`. Phase E adds
`TrackRecordAwareDefensibilityJudge` for non-fallback Type B estimators
(Method-fit / Robustness from FS10's track records). Type A continues to
use the stub until a real LLM-graded scorer lands.

Storage-dependent submodules (store, gating_store,
research_defensibility_store, track_record_loader, track_record_aware_judge,
factory) require FS11 DB schema rows and FS10 track-record infrastructure
that land in a separate port PR. They are imported lazily so that the
core scorer protocols remain importable without those dependencies.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from .defensibility_judge import (
    DefensibilityJudge,
    StubDefensibilityJudge,
    default_defensibility_judge,
)

# Storage-dependent submodules are not eagerly imported. Downstream code
# that needs them should import from the submodule directly, e.g.:
#   from packages.domain.qc.factory import build_judge
```

"""FS10 validation per-component artifact builders.

Each module here owns the artifact emitted by one feature-set's component
during a validation run. Per FS10 §4 / §6, the validation harness collects
these per-FS artifacts so the cross-FS failure-distribution analytics can
attribute issues to the right upstream layer.

Phase D scope: adds `baseline_estimation` (FS4) artifact emission.
Subsequent phases add per-FS modules as their components ship.
"""

from .baseline_estimation import (
    BaselineEstimationArtifact,
    NodeBaselineRecord,
    build_baseline_estimation_artifact,
)
from .fs3_extraction import (
    ExtractionArtifact,
    ExtractionNodeRecord,
    build_driver_extraction_artifact,
    build_indicator_extraction_artifact,
)
from .fs5_edge_weights import (
    EdgeWeightArtifact,
    EdgeWeightRecord,
    build_edge_weight_artifact,
)
from .fs6_inference import (
    PosteriorInferenceArtifact,
    SensitivityAnalysisArtifact,
    SensitivityIndicatorRecord,
    TargetPosteriorRecord,
    build_posterior_inference_artifact,
    build_sensitivity_analysis_artifact,
)
from .fs7_summary import (
    QuestionSummaryArtifact,
    build_question_summary_artifact,
)

__all__ = [
    "BaselineEstimationArtifact",
    "NodeBaselineRecord",
    "ExtractionArtifact",
    "ExtractionNodeRecord",
    "EdgeWeightArtifact",
    "EdgeWeightRecord",
    "PosteriorInferenceArtifact",
    "QuestionSummaryArtifact",
    "SensitivityAnalysisArtifact",
    "SensitivityIndicatorRecord",
    "TargetPosteriorRecord",
    "build_baseline_estimation_artifact",
    "build_driver_extraction_artifact",
    "build_indicator_extraction_artifact",
    "build_edge_weight_artifact",
    "build_posterior_inference_artifact",
    "build_sensitivity_analysis_artifact",
    "build_question_summary_artifact",
]

> AGENT

Research complete. Here's the Phase 2 harmonization and Phase 3 proposal.

---

## SPI-232 Phase 2: Harmonization

**Source artifacts:** SPI-232 ticket, `docs/plans/FS3/` design docs, `docs/plans/definitions/graph-entity-definitions.md`, `promotion-graph-apply-contracts.md`.

### Dependency map

| File | On dev? | Dependencies satisfied? |
|---|---|---|
| `packages/domain/graph_entity_validation.py` | MISSING | Yes (`packages.storage.EdgeRecord, NodeRecord`) |
| `fs10_validation/components/fs3_extraction.py` | MISSING | Yes (`core.node_data.node_detail`) |
| `tests/test_graph_entity_validation.py` | MISSING | Yes (once graph_entity_validation.py lands) |
| `tests/test_fs3_qc.py` | MISSING | Yes (all QC functions exist on dev) |
| `tests/test_fs3_upstream_concerns.py` | MISSING | Yes |
| `fs10_validation/graph_construction.py` | MISSING | **NO** — needs `component_store`, `run_store`, `validation_data_store` (all missing, SPI-230 scope) |
| `tests/test_fs3_validation_replay.py` | MISSING | **NO** — needs `fs10_validation.component_store` (SPI-230) |

### Changes to existing files

| File | Diff lines | Nature |
|---|---|---|
| `indicator_graph_apply.py` | 487 | FS3-scoped: REFERENCE edges from info->indicator, per-driver indicator identity keys, driver matching by ID, document key matching |
| `test_indicator_graph_apply.py` | 582 | Corresponding test updates |
| `driver_indicator_literature.py` | 206 | Lazy import refactor (import-time isolation) |
| `indicator_extraction.py` | 100 | Extraction improvements |
| `driver_promotion_tool.py` | 49 | Minor changes |
| `driver_promotion.py` (routes) | 24 | Minor changes |
| `test_insight_agent_graph_apply.py` | 20 | Minor test updates |
| `test_driver_promotion.py` | 0 | Identical |
| `test_insight_agent_graph_contracts.py` | 0 | Identical |

### `fs10_validation/components/__init__.py` issue

On dev_sprint, this `__init__.py` imports from 5 component modules (baseline_estimation, fs3_extraction, fs5_edge_weights, fs6_inference, fs7_summary). Only `fs3_extraction` is in SPI-232 scope — the other 4 belong to other tickets. We'd need a **minimal `__init__.py`** that only exports FS3 extraction, with the others added by their respective tickets later.

### Findings

1. **Design-implementation alignment is good.** The dev_sprint code matches the FS3 design docs for extraction, indicator scoping, graph admission, and edge creation.

2. **Two files are blocked** — `graph_construction.py` and `test_fs3_validation_replay.py` both depend on FS10 validation stores that don't exist on dev yet (SPI-230 scope). These must be deferred.

3. **No contradictions** between design and implementation.

4. **The `indicator_graph_apply.py` diff is substantial but FS3-scoped** — adds REFERENCE edge creation from information nodes to indicators, per-driver indicator identity keys (preventing cross-driver dedup), and document key matching for source attribution. All directly FS3 graph admission improvements.

---

## Phase 3: Proposed approach

### In scope

1. **Create new files:**
   - `packages/domain/graph_entity_validation.py` — graph admission gate
   - `fs10_validation/components/__init__.py` — minimal, FS3-only exports
   - `fs10_validation/components/fs3_extraction.py` — extraction artifacts
   - `tests/test_graph_entity_validation.py`
   - `tests/test_fs3_qc.py`
   - `tests/test_fs3_upstream_concerns.py`

2. **Port changes to existing files:**
   - `core/research_driver/indicator_graph_apply.py` (REFERENCE edges, identity keys, doc matching)
   - `tests/test_indicator_graph_apply.py` (corresponding tests)
   - `core/research_driver/driver_indicator_literature.py` (lazy imports)
   - `core/research_driver/indicator_extraction.py`
   - `core/research_driver/driver_promotion_tool.py`
   - `services/api/routes/driver_promotion.py`
   - `tests/test_insight_agent_graph_apply.py`

### Out of scope (deliberately)

- `fs10_validation/graph_construction.py` — blocked on SPI-230 (FS10 validation stores)
- `tests/test_fs3_validation_replay.py` — blocked on SPI-230 (`component_store`)
- Other `fs10_validation/components/` modules (baseline_estimation, fs5_edge_weights, fs6_inference, fs7_summary) — belong to their respective FS tickets

### Approach

Direct port from dev_sprint for new files. Apply dev_sprint diffs to existing files. The `components/__init__.py` will be trimmed to only export FS3 extraction. All acceptance criteria that can run will be run; the two blocked tests will be explicitly called out.

---

**Phase 3 gate: Do you approve this approach?**

> DEVELOPER

sorry i have no context what is this ticket for

> AGENT

Fair. Let me cut through the jargon.

**What the system does today on `dev`:** The research pipeline reads evidence documents, extracts "drivers" (causal factors that affect a question's outcome) and "indicators" (observable signals that predict driver activity), then wires them into a Bayesian graph with edges.

**What's missing on `dev` that `dev_sprint` has:**

1. **Graph structure validation** (`graph_entity_validation.py`) — Rules that reject malformed graphs before they're saved. E.g., "an indicator must connect to a driver, not directly to a target" or "no duplicate target nodes." Right now dev has no gate — anything gets written.

2. **Better indicator→driver wiring** (changes to `indicator_graph_apply.py`) — On dev_sprint, when an indicator is extracted from a driver's literature, it creates a REFERENCE edge back to the source document and scopes the indicator to that specific driver. On dev, indicators can accidentally deduplicate across drivers and source attribution is weaker.

3. **Extraction quality scoring** (`fs3_extraction.py`) — A component that counts how many drivers/indicators are evidence-backed vs LLM-generated fallbacks, so FS10 validation can score extraction quality.

4. **QC pipeline tests** — Tests proving that the quality control system can score FS3 outputs and flag weak extractions for analyst review.

**In one sentence:** This ticket adds the "are the extracted drivers and indicators structurally valid and traceable?" layer that's currently missing on `dev`.

The changes to driver_promotion and indicator_extraction are minor improvements that came along in the same dev_sprint work.

Want me to proceed with the approach I outlined, or do you want to scope it differently?

> DEVELOPER

Okay, so basically this ticket that we're looking at on this track, or this sorry this lane, lane one, the current ticket is just about making sure that graphs aren't malformed. Is that it? And what's was before in the track and what comes after in the track?

> AGENT

**Lane 1 track:**

1. **SPI-227** (DONE, just merged) — Document storage authority. Gives the system artifact-backed document byte storage so evidence files are tracked with SHA256 hashes and locators in the DB instead of loose files.

2. **SPI-232** (current) — FS3 extraction, graph admission, document-pack validation.

3. Nothing after — lane 1 ends here.

**Is SPI-232 just about malformed graphs?**

No, that's one piece. It's actually three things bundled:

1. **Graph admission gate** — yes, reject malformed graphs (the `graph_entity_validation.py` module). This is the most self-contained piece.

2. **Stronger extraction wiring** — improvements to how indicators get linked to their parent drivers and source documents. This is changes to existing files (`indicator_graph_apply.py` etc.) that make the extraction pipeline more precise about provenance.

3. **Extraction quality measurement** — a new FS10 validation component (`fs3_extraction.py`) that scores how good the extraction was (evidence-backed vs LLM-generated fallback counts). This feeds into the validation/QC system.

So SPI-227 was "store documents properly" and SPI-232 is "extract entities from those documents into the graph properly and validate the result." They're sequential because you need document storage authority before you can validate what was extracted from those documents.

The ticket is broader than just malformed graph rejection — it's the full "extraction quality and correctness" layer. Want me to proceed, or does the scope feel too wide for one PR?

> DEVELOPER

wtf is this for def _get_run_agent_single_scope_retrieval() -> Any:
    global run_agent_single_scope_retrieval
    if run_agent_single_scope_retrieval is None:

> AGENT

That's a lazy import pattern. On dev, `driver_indicator_literature.py` imports these at the top of the file:

```python
from core.research_driver.literature_retrieval_runtime import run_agent_single_scope_retrieval
from services.worker.agents.ResearchDriver.research_agent.agent import LiteratureEvidenceRetrievalAgent
from services.worker.agents.ResearchDriver.research_agent.config import (
    EvidenceDownloadConfig,
    LiteratureEvidenceRetrievalConfig,
    LiteratureQueryConfig,
)
```

The dev_sprint version wraps each of these in a `_get_*()` function so they're only imported when actually called at runtime, not at import time. This means test files can `import driver_indicator_literature` without pulling in the entire worker agent stack and its heavy dependencies (LiteLLM, crawl infrastructure, etc.).

It's an import-time isolation refactor — makes the module testable without loading everything. Not FS3 functionality, just infrastructure cleanup that came along in the same dev_sprint work.

Honestly it's a bit ugly. If it bothers you I can revert that file to the dev version — the FS3 functional changes don't depend on it.

> DEVELOPER

is it a bad pattern, do we need it?

> AGENT

It's not a great pattern — globals, mutation, `Any` return types. The standard Python way to do this is just put the import inside the function that needs it:

```python
def some_function_that_needs_it():
    from core.research_driver.literature_retrieval_runtime import run_agent_single_scope_retrieval
    ...
```

As for whether we need it at all — let me check if any of our SPI-232 tests actually fail without it.

---

## Code Quality Review: `port/fs3-extraction-graph-admission`

I've completed a thorough review of all changes in this branch. Below is my detailed assessment organized by file.

---

### **File: `core/research_driver/driver_indicator_literature.py`**

**Status: NEEDS FIX**

**Issues:**

1. **Global mutation pattern (lines 40-44, 47-82)** - MAJOR
   - Lazy-loading imports via global mutation in `_get_run_agent_single_scope_retrieval()` and `_get_retrieval_components()` is an anti-pattern that makes reasoning about code flow difficult.
   - Uses mutable global state to cache module imports, which can hide failures and cause problems in threaded/async contexts.
   - The `_get_retrieval_components()` function has complex branching logic across separate global state checks (lines 61-65) making it fragile.

2. **Overly defensive string conversions (line 244)** - MINOR
   - `str(driver.get("driver_id") or "").strip()` - defensive pattern used everywhere but the empty string default is redundant with `.strip()` behavior.

3. **Silent exception catch (line 451)** - MEDIUM
   - Line 451: `except Exception as exc:` is bare-except pattern that silently swallows errors. While there's a log, this masks upstream issues.

4. **State mutation in early return (lines 404-421)** - MEDIUM
   - The early return path that writes `result.json` before attempting retrieval works but the code path split makes it harder to trace the happy path. Increases cognitive complexity.

**Verdict: NEEDS FIX** - The global mutation pattern is a code smell that violates clean code principles. Recommend refactoring lazy-loading imports into a proper dependency injection or module-level initialization at import time.

---

### **File: `core/research_driver/driver_promotion_tool.py`**

**Status: CLEAN**

**Observations:**
- Embedded system prompt as a string constant (`_SYSTEM_PROMPT`) instead of loading from external file is reasonable for a simple static prompt.
- Line 72: Assigns `str(model or "").strip()` to a local variable, though this pattern seems slightly overcautious when the string is only used once.
- Removed two unused imports (`resolve_role_model`, `default_prompt_store`), which is good cleanup.

**Verdict: CLEAN**

---

### **File: `core/research_driver/indicator_extraction.py`**

**Status: MINOR ISSUES**

**Issues:**

1. **Conditional proxy logic (lines 205-211 in diff)** - MINOR
   - `**({} if os.environ.get("LITELLM_PROXY_BASE_URL") else {"cache_control": {"type": "ephemeral"}})` - Magic environment variable check embedded in two places (lines 211, 393).
   - This pattern is fragile: the condition is not documented and couples prompt building to deployment infrastructure. Should be centralized or documented.

2. **Removed function now inlined (lines 413-420)** - MODERATE
   - The old `_load_system_prompt()` was removed and calls were replaced with direct string constant `_SYSTEM_PROMPT`.
   - The `run()` method now calls `self._runtime.complete_structured()` instead of the direct `extract_causal_relationships()` path. This is a significant logic change that affects behavior.
   - However, this refactoring appears intentional (moving to shared proxy-safe LLM runtime). The comment explains it.

3. **INDICATOR_EXTRACTION_MODEL export (line 42)** - OK
   - Added as a compatibility alias for legacy importers. This is appropriate for backward compatibility.

**Verdict: MINOR ISSUES** - The proxy environment variable check embedded in code is the main concern. Consider extracting to a helper function with a clear name.

---

### **File: `core/research_driver/indicator_graph_apply.py`**

**Status: CLEAN**

**Observations:**
- Large diff with significant new functionality: reference edge creation, driver-scoped indicator identity keys, information source lookup.
- **Well-structured new code:** Functions like `_indicator_identity_key()`, `_source_keys_from_payload()`, `_information_source_lookup()`, and `_resolved_information_ids_for_sources()` are all well-defined with clear purposes.
- **Identity key change (lines 105-110):** Moved from simple normalized label to composite driver_id + label key. This is intentional to support driver-scoped indicators and is well-justified.
- **Legacy key fallback (lines 414-417):** Gracefully handles migration from old identity key scheme. Good design.
- **Stage outcome logic refactored (lines 636-704):** Logic for determining partial vs. failed status was reordered. The new logic checks `review_required_no_candidates` before `missing_indicators`. Appears correct and addresses a specific case.
- **No global mutation, no bare excepts, no dead code.**

**Verdict: CLEAN** - This is well-structured, intentional refactoring with good separation of concerns.

---

### **File: `fs10_validation/components/__init__.py`**

**Status: CLEAN**

- Straightforward module initialization with clear public API exports.
- Properly documents the module purpose.

**Verdict: CLEAN**

---

### **File: `fs10_validation/components/fs3_extraction.py`**

**Status: CLEAN**

**Observations:**
- Well-designed dataclass structure for artifact emission.
- Functions like `_is_grounded()`, `_uses_fallback()` have clear single responsibilities.
- Uses frozen dataclasses with slots (good for immutability and memory).
- Property methods (`grounded_count`, `fallback_count`) compute aggregates appropriately.
- No dead code, no bare excepts, all imports are used.

**Verdict: CLEAN**

---

### **File: `packages/domain/graph_entity_validation.py`**

**Status: CLEAN**

**Observations:**
- New validation module with well-defined constants (`CANONICAL_NODE_TYPES`, `VALID_CONDITIONAL_ENDPOINTS`, etc.) at module level.
- Helper functions (`_node_type()`, `_node_detail()`, `_edge_type()`) are small and focused.
- The main validation function `validate_graph_entities()` is large (~150 lines) but appropriately structured with clear sections for different validation concerns.
- All imports are used, no global mutation, no silent failures.

**Verdict: CLEAN**

---

### **File: `services/api/routes/driver_promotion.py`**

### **File: `services/worker/agents/ResearchDriver/insight_agent/graph_apply.py`**

**Status: CLEAN**

- Added `search_query` extraction (line 206).
- Added `reference_type="source"` to edge creation (line 316).
- All changes are small, localized, and intentional. Consistent with changes in indicator_graph_apply.py.

### **Test Files**

**Status: CLEAN**

**Observations:**

- **`test_fs3_qc.py`**: New test file with comprehensive coverage of QC persistence and gate review workflows. No issues.
- **`test_fs3_upstream_concerns.py`**: Tests concern traceability in database rows. Appropriately focused.
- **`test_graph_entity_validation.py`**: Tests all major validation paths including edge cases. Well-structured test cases.
- **`test_indicator_graph_apply.py`**: Large test additions (503 insertions). 
  - New test `test_apply_indicators_links_source_backed_information_to_indicator()` validates reference edge creation.
  - New test `test_apply_indicators_marks_all_review_required_no_candidate_rows_as_partial()` validates partial status logic.
  - New test `test_apply_indicators_marks_missing_driver_nodes_as_partial_when_matched_rows_succeed()` validates missing driver node handling.
  - New test `test_apply_indicators_matches_graph_admitted_driver_id_before_title()` validates driver ID matching priority.
  - Test rename: `test_shared_indicator_across_drivers_creates_one_node_two_edges()` → `test_same_indicator_name_across_drivers_creates_driver_scoped_nodes()` reflects the intentional behavior change from shared to driver-scoped indicators.
  - Mock refactoring in `test_claude_api_mock_end_to_end()` changed from mocking `_get_litellm_completion_fn` to mocking `LLMRuntime.complete_structured()`. This aligns with the shift to shared proxy-safe runtime.

- **`test_insight_agent_graph_apply.py`**: Small additions validating `reference_type="source"` on reference edges. Appropriate.

All tests follow existing patterns and are well-structured. **Verdict: CLEAN**

> AGENT

`EdgeRecord.type` is `str | None`. So `edge.type` can legitimately be `None` — that's why dev_sprint added the defensive check. But `str(edge.type or "").strip().upper()` is ridiculous when you can just write `(edge.type or "") == "CONDITIONAL"` or even better `edge.type == "CONDITIONAL"` since any edge with a null type isn't CONDITIONAL anyway.

Revert to the dev version?
