1	"""Validation helpers for persisted graph entity definitions."""
2	
3	from __future__ import annotations
4	
5	from collections import defaultdict
6	from collections.abc import Iterable, Mapping
7	from dataclasses import dataclass
8	from typing import Any
9	
10	from packages.storage import EdgeRecord, NodeRecord
11	
12	CANONICAL_NODE_TYPES = {"target", "driver", "indicator", "information"}
13	LEGACY_NODE_TYPE_ALIASES = {"option": "target", "evidence": "information"}
14	VALID_CONDITIONAL_ENDPOINTS = {
15	    ("indicator", "driver"),
16	    ("indicator", "target"),
17	    ("driver", "driver"),
18	    ("driver", "target"),
19	}
20	VALID_REFERENCE_ENDPOINTS = {
21	    ("information", "indicator"),
22	    ("information", "driver"),
23	}
24	VALID_REFERENCE_KINDS = {
25	    ("information", "indicator"): {"source", "evidence"},
26	    ("information", "driver"): {"source", "evidence"},
27	}
28	VALID_GRAPH_KINDS = {"generic", "driver_indicator", "full"}
29	
30	
31	@dataclass(frozen=True)
32	class GraphEntityValidationIssue:
33	    code: str
34	    message: str
35	    subject_id: str
36	    details: dict[str, Any]
37	
38	
39	def _mapping(value: Any) -> dict[str, Any]:
40	    if isinstance(value, Mapping):
41	        return dict(value)
42	    return {}
43	
44	
45	def _node_type(node: NodeRecord) -> str:
46	    raw_type = str(node.type or "").strip().lower()
47	    return LEGACY_NODE_TYPE_ALIASES.get(raw_type, raw_type)
48	
49	
50	def _node_question_id(node: NodeRecord) -> str:
51	    return str(_mapping(node.data).get("question_id") or "").strip()
52	
53	
54	def _node_detail(node: NodeRecord) -> dict[str, Any]:
55	    data = _mapping(node.data)
56	    detail = _mapping(data.get("detail"))
57	    return detail or data
58	
59	
60	def _edge_detail(edge: EdgeRecord) -> dict[str, Any]:
61	    data = _mapping(edge.data)
62	    detail = _mapping(data.get("detail"))
63	    return detail or data
64	
65	
66	def _edge_type(edge: EdgeRecord) -> str:
67	    return str(edge.type or "").strip().upper()
68	
69	
70	def _edge_question_id(edge: EdgeRecord) -> str:
71	    return str(_mapping(edge.data).get("question_id") or "").strip()
72	
73	
74	def _is_question_definition_driver(node: NodeRecord) -> bool:
75	    if str(node.type or "").strip().lower() != "driver":
76	        return False
77	    origin = _mapping(_mapping(node.data).get("origin"))
78	    return origin.get("stage") == "question_definition"
79	
80	
81	def _normalize_graph_kind(graph_kind: str) -> str:
82	    resolved = str(graph_kind or "generic").strip().lower() or "generic"
83	    if resolved not in VALID_GRAPH_KINDS:
84	        raise ValueError(f"Unsupported graph_kind {graph_kind!r}; expected one of {sorted(VALID_GRAPH_KINDS)}")
85	    return resolved
86	
87	
88	def validate_graph_entities(
89	    *,
90	    nodes: Iterable[NodeRecord],
91	    edges: Iterable[EdgeRecord],
92	    question_ids: Iterable[str] | None = None,
93	    graph_kind: str = "generic",
94	) -> list[GraphEntityValidationIssue]:
95	    """Validate graph records against canonical graph entity definitions.
96	
97	    The validator is intentionally opt-in so legacy fixtures can be repaired
98	    before these checks become a global write barrier.
99	    """
100	    resolved_graph_kind = _normalize_graph_kind(graph_kind)
101	    allowed_question_ids = {str(question_id).strip() for question_id in question_ids or [] if str(question_id).strip()}
102	    node_list = list(nodes)
103	    edge_list = list(edges)
104	    node_by_id = {str(node.node_id): node for node in node_list}
105	    relevant_node_ids = {
106	        str(node.node_id) for node in node_list if not allowed_question_ids or _node_question_id(node) in allowed_question_ids
107	    }
108	    issues: list[GraphEntityValidationIssue] = []
109	
110	    target_groups: dict[tuple[str, int], list[str]] = defaultdict(list)
111	    target_ids_by_question: dict[str, set[str]] = defaultdict(set)
112	    indicator_driver_edges: dict[str, set[str]] = defaultdict(set)
113	    driver_target_edges: dict[tuple[str, str], set[str]] = defaultdict(set)
114	
115	    for node in node_list:
116	        node_id = str(node.node_id)
117	        if allowed_question_ids and _node_question_id(node) not in allowed_question_ids:
118	            continue
119	        raw_type = str(node.type or "").strip().lower()
120	        canonical_type = _node_type(node)
121	        if raw_type == "question":
122	            issues.append(
123	                GraphEntityValidationIssue(
124	                    code="persisted_question_node",
125	                    message="Questions must live in the questions table, not persisted graph nodes.",
126	                    subject_id=node_id,
127	                    details={"node_type": raw_type, "question_id": _node_question_id(node)},
128	                )
129	            )
130	        elif raw_type == "driver" and _is_question_definition_driver(node):
131	            issues.append(
132	                GraphEntityValidationIssue(
133	                    code="qnode_as_driver",
134	                    message="Question-definition scaffolding must not be persisted as a driver node.",
135	                    subject_id=node_id,
136	                    details={"node_type": raw_type, "question_id": _node_question_id(node)},
137	                )
138	            )
139	        elif canonical_type not in CANONICAL_NODE_TYPES:
140	            issues.append(
141	                GraphEntityValidationIssue(
142	                    code="unknown_node_type",
143	                    message="Node type is not part of the canonical graph entity definitions.",
144	                    subject_id=node_id,
145	                    details={"node_type": raw_type, "canonical_type": canonical_type},
146	                )
147	            )
148	
149	        if canonical_type == "target":
150	            target_ids_by_question[_node_question_id(node)].add(node_id)
151	            detail = _node_detail(node)
152	            option_index = detail.get("option_index")
153	            if isinstance(option_index, int):
154	                target_groups[(_node_question_id(node), option_index)].append(node_id)
155	
156	    for (question_id, option_index), target_ids in sorted(target_groups.items()):
157	        if len(target_ids) <= 1:
158	            continue
159	        issues.append(
160	            GraphEntityValidationIssue(
161	                code="duplicate_target_option",
162	                message="A question may have only one target node per option index.",
163	                subject_id=f"{question_id}:{option_index}",
164	                details={"question_id": question_id, "option_index": option_index, "target_ids": target_ids},
165	            )
166	        )
167	
168	    for edge in edge_list:
169	        edge_id = str(edge.edge_id)
170	        if allowed_question_ids:
171	            edge_qid = _edge_question_id(edge)
172	            if (
173	                edge_qid not in allowed_question_ids
174	                and str(edge.from_node) not in relevant_node_ids
175	                and str(edge.to_node) not in relevant_node_ids
176	            ):
177	                continue
178	        from_node = node_by_id.get(str(edge.from_node))
179	        to_node = node_by_id.get(str(edge.to_node))
180	        if from_node is None or to_node is None:
181	            issues.append(
182	                GraphEntityValidationIssue(
183	                    code="edge_missing_endpoint",
184	                    message="Edge endpoint is missing from the graph node set.",
185	                    subject_id=edge_id,
186	                    details={"from_node": edge.from_node, "to_node": edge.to_node},
187	                )
188	            )
189	            continue
190	        from_type = _node_type(from_node)
191	        to_type = _node_type(to_node)
192	        pair = (from_type, to_type)
193	        edge_type = _edge_type(edge)
194	        if edge_type == "CONDITIONAL":
195	            if pair not in VALID_CONDITIONAL_ENDPOINTS:
196	                issues.append(
197	                    GraphEntityValidationIssue(
198	                        code="invalid_conditional_endpoint_pair",
199	                        message="CONDITIONAL edges use an invalid endpoint pair.",
200	                        subject_id=edge_id,
201	                        details={"from_type": from_type, "to_type": to_type},
202	                    )
203	                )
204	            elif pair == ("indicator", "driver"):
205	                indicator_driver_edges[str(edge.from_node)].add(str(edge.to_node))
206	            elif resolved_graph_kind == "full" and pair == ("driver", "target"):
207	                question_id = _node_question_id(to_node) or _node_question_id(from_node) or _edge_question_id(edge)
208	                driver_target_edges[(question_id, str(edge.from_node))].add(str(edge.to_node))
209	            continue
210	        if edge_type == "REFERENCE":
211	            if pair == ("driver", "target"):
212	                issues.append(
213	                    GraphEntityValidationIssue(
214	                        code="driver_target_reference_edge",
215	                        message="driver -> target edges must be CONDITIONAL, not REFERENCE.",
216	                        subject_id=edge_id,
217	                        details={"from_type": from_type, "to_type": to_type},
218	                    )
219	                )
220	                continue
221	            if pair == ("information", "target"):
222	                issues.append(
223	                    GraphEntityValidationIssue(
224	                        code="information_target_reference_edge",
225	                        message="Information nodes must reference drivers or indicators, not targets.",
226	                        subject_id=edge_id,
227	                        details={"from_type": from_type, "to_type": to_type},
228	                    )
229	                )
230	                continue
231	            if pair not in VALID_REFERENCE_ENDPOINTS:
232	                issues.append(
233	                    GraphEntityValidationIssue(
234	                        code="invalid_reference_endpoint_pair",
235	                        message="REFERENCE edges use an invalid endpoint pair.",
236	                        subject_id=edge_id,
237	                        details={"from_type": from_type, "to_type": to_type},
238	                    )
239	                )
240	                continue
241	            reference_type = str(_edge_detail(edge).get("reference_type") or "").strip().lower()
242	            if reference_type and reference_type not in VALID_REFERENCE_KINDS[pair]:
243	                issues.append(
244	                    GraphEntityValidationIssue(
245	                        code="invalid_reference_kind",
246	                        message="REFERENCE edge kind is not valid for its endpoint pair.",
247	                        subject_id=edge_id,
248	                        details={"from_type": from_type, "to_type": to_type, "reference_type": reference_type},
249	                    )
250	                )
251	            continue
252	        issues.append(
253	            GraphEntityValidationIssue(
254	                code="unknown_edge_type",
255	                message="Edge type is not part of the canonical graph entity definitions.",
256	                subject_id=edge_id,
257	                details={"edge_type": edge_type},
258	            )
259	        )
260	
261	    for indicator_id, driver_ids in sorted(indicator_driver_edges.items()):
262	        if len(driver_ids) <= 1:
263	            continue
264	        issues.append(
265	            GraphEntityValidationIssue(
266	                code="shared_indicator_multiple_drivers",
267	                message="Indicator nodes must be scoped to one parent driver, not shared across multiple drivers.",
268	                subject_id=indicator_id,
269	                details={"indicator_id": indicator_id, "driver_ids": sorted(driver_ids)},
270	            )
271	        )
272	
273	    if resolved_graph_kind == "full":
274	        for (question_id, driver_id), actual_target_ids in sorted(driver_target_edges.items()):
275	            expected_target_ids = set(target_ids_by_question.get(question_id) or set())
276	            if not expected_target_ids:
277	                issues.append(
278	                    GraphEntityValidationIssue(
279	                        code="driver_target_without_question_targets",
280	                        message="A driver -> target CONDITIONAL edge must belong to a question with target nodes.",
281	                        subject_id=driver_id,
282	                        details={
283	                            "question_id": question_id,
284	                            "driver_id": driver_id,
285	                            "actual_target_ids": sorted(actual_target_ids),
286	                        },
287	                    )
288	                )
289	                continue
290	            missing_target_ids = sorted(expected_target_ids - actual_target_ids)
291	            extra_target_ids = sorted(actual_target_ids - expected_target_ids)
292	            if missing_target_ids or extra_target_ids:
293	                issues.append(
294	                    GraphEntityValidationIssue(
295	                        code="incomplete_driver_target_fanout",
296	                        message="A driver with one target edge must have CONDITIONAL edges to every target for the question.",
297	                        subject_id=driver_id,
298	                        details={
299	                            "question_id": question_id,
300	                            "driver_id": driver_id,
301	                            "expected_target_ids": sorted(expected_target_ids),
302	                            "actual_target_ids": sorted(actual_target_ids),
303	                            "missing_target_ids": missing_target_ids,
304	                            "extra_target_ids": extra_target_ids,
305	                        },
306	                    )
307	                )
308	
309	    return issues
310

5	from dataclasses import asdict, dataclass, field

13	@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)

15	    node_id: str
16	    node_label: str
17	    role: NodeRole
18	    source_mode: str
19	    generation_mode: str
20	    grounding_status: str
21	    epistemic_status: str

26

28	        return asdict(self)
29	
30	
31	@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
32	class ExtractionArtifact:
33	    question_id: str
34	    role: NodeRole
35	    records: list[ExtractionNodeRecord] = field(default_factory=list)
36	
37	    @property
38	    def grounded_count(self) -> int:
39	        return sum(1 for record in self.records if _is_grounded(record))

41	    @property

48	
49	    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
50	        return {
51	            "question_id": self.question_id,
52	            "role": self.role,
53	            "record_count": len(self.records),
54	            "grounded_count": self.grounded_count,
55	            "fallback_count": self.fallback_count,
56	            "review_required_count": self.review_required_count,
57	            "records": [record.to_dict() for record in self.records],
58	        }
59	
60	
61	def build_driver_extraction_artifact(
62	    *,
63	    nodes: list[Any],
64	    question_id: str,
65	) -> ExtractionArtifact:
66	    return ExtractionArtifact(
67	        question_id=question_id,
68	        role="driver",
69	        records=[_record_from_node(node, "driver") for node in nodes if _node_role(node) == "driver"],

74	    *,

85	def _node_role(node: Any) -> NodeRole | None:
86	    raw = str(getattr(node, "type", "") or "").strip().lower()
87	    if raw in {"driver", "indicator"}:
88	        return raw  # type: ignore[return-value]
89	    return None
90	
91	
92	def _node_id(node: Any) -> str:
93	    raw = getattr(node, "id", None)
94	    if raw in (None, ""):
95	        raw = getattr(node, "node_id", "")
96	    return str(raw or "").strip()
97	
98	
99	def _detail(node: Any) -> dict[str, Any]:
100	    return dict(node_detail(getattr(node, "data", {}) or {}) or {})
101	
102	
103	def _quality_flags(detail: dict[str, Any]) -> list[str]:
104	    raw = detail.get("quality_flags")
105	    if not isinstance(raw, list):
106	        return []
107	    return sorted({str(item).strip() for item in raw if str(item).strip()})
108	
109	
110	def _concern_patterns(detail: dict[str, Any]) -> list[str]:
111	    patterns: set[str] = set()
112	    for key in ("concerns", "baseline_concerns", "estimation_concerns"):
113	        raw = detail.get(key)
114	        if not isinstance(raw, list):
115	            continue
116	        for item in raw:
117	            if not isinstance(item, dict):
118	                continue
119	            pattern = str(item.get("detected_pattern") or "").strip()
120	            if pattern:
121	                patterns.add(pattern)
122	    baseline = detail.get("baseline")
123	    if isinstance(baseline, dict):
124	        raw = baseline.get("concerns")
125	        if isinstance(raw, list):
126	            for item in raw:
127	                if not isinstance(item, dict):
128	                    continue
129	                pattern = str(item.get("detected_pattern") or "").strip()
130	                if pattern:
131	                    patterns.add(pattern)
132	    return sorted(patterns)
133	
134	
135	def _record_from_node(node: Any, role: NodeRole) -> ExtractionNodeRecord:
136	    detail = _detail(node)
137	    source_contexts = getattr(node, "data", {}).get("source_contexts") or []
138	    return ExtractionNodeRecord(
139	        node_id=_node_id(node),
140	        node_label=str(getattr(node, "label", "") or "").strip(),
141	        role=role,
142	        source_mode=str(detail.get("source_mode") or "").strip(),
143	        generation_mode=str(detail.get("generation_mode") or "").strip(),
144	        grounding_status=str(detail.get("grounding_status") or "").strip(),
145	        epistemic_status=str(detail.get("epistemic_status") or "").strip(),
146	        source_context_count=len(source_contexts) if isinstance(source_contexts, list) else 0,
147	        review_required=bool(detail.get("review_required")),
148	        quality_flags=_quality_flags(detail),
149	        concern_patterns=_concern_patterns(detail),
150	    )
151	
152	
153	def _is_grounded(record: ExtractionNodeRecord) -> bool:
154	    if record.source_context_count > 0:
155	        return True
156	    return record.grounding_status not in {"", "ungrounded", "llm_compensated"}
157	
158	
159	def _uses_fallback(record: ExtractionNodeRecord) -> bool:
160	    source_mode = record.source_mode.lower()
161	    generation_mode = record.generation_mode.lower()
162	    if "fallback" in source_mode or "fallback" in generation_mode:
163	        return True
164	    if "free_knowledge" in source_mode or "free_knowledge" in generation_mode:
165	        return True
166	    return record.grounding_status == "llm_compensated"
167	
168	
169	__all__ = [
170	    "ExtractionArtifact",
171	    "ExtractionNodeRecord",
172	    "build_driver_extraction_artifact",
173	    "build_indicator_extraction_artifact",
174	]
175

> AGENT

The PR is clean. Here's the full verdict:

**`driver_promotion_tool.py`** — `str(model or "").strip()` is NOT a bug. `_resolve_model()` (line 1144) falls through to `task_id="FS8.driver_promotion"` when model is empty, which calls `resolve_task_model()`. The dev version's `model or resolve_role_model("tool")` would actually skip task-based model resolution — the dev_sprint version is slightly better here.

**`indicator_extraction.py`** — Clean. Proxy check is correct. `run()` via `_runtime.complete_structured()` is fine.

**`indicator_graph_apply.py`** — Clean. `reference_edge_type`, `_indicator_identity_key()`, REFERENCE edge creation, all solid.

**`graph_entity_validation.py`** — Clean.

**`fs3_extraction.py`** — Clean.

---

**Summary of what was found and fixed in this PR:**

| Issue | File | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Global mutation lazy import pattern | `driver_indicator_literature.py` | Reverted to dev |
| `str(edge.type or "").strip().upper()` defensive nonsense | `driver_promotion.py` (route) | Reverted to dev |
| Missing `source_index`/`search_query`/`reference_type` keys | `services/.../graph_apply.py` | Fixed (was causing `KeyError` in tests) |

Everything else is clean. The PR is in good shape for review.

"""
LLM tool for proposing sub-question targets when promoting a driver.

Given a driver's context and its parent question, proposes a structured
set of MECE outcome options suitable for a standalone sub-question.

Where It Is Used:
- services.api.routes.driver_promotion

Key Symbols:
- DriverPromotionProposalTool
- propose_driver_promotion
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import logging
from typing import Any

from pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict, Field

from packages.inference.llm import BaseLLMTool, LLMConfig
from packages.inference.llm.tiers import resolve_role_model
from packages.inference.prompts import default_prompt_store

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


def _load_system_prompt() -> str:
    return default_prompt_store().load("FS8/driver_promotion/system.md")


class _ProposedOption(BaseModel):
    model_config = ConfigDict(extra="ignore")

    label: str = Field(description="Outcome option label")
    description: str = Field(default="", description="What this outcome means")
    suggested_baseline_p: float | None = Field(
        default=None,
        description="Suggested prior probability [0.01, 0.99]",
    )


class _DriverPromotionLLMOutput(BaseModel):
    model_config = ConfigDict(extra="ignore")

    question_label: str = Field(description="Proposed question text")
    classification: str = Field(
        description="One of: binary, categorical, numeric_bracket",
    )
    options: list[_ProposedOption] = Field(
        description="2-7 MECE outcome options",
    )
    rationale: str = Field(default="", description="Why this decomposition makes sense")


class DriverPromotionProposalTool(BaseLLMTool[dict[str, Any]]):
    response_model = _DriverPromotionLLMOutput

    def __init__(self, *, model: str | None = None) -> None:

"""
Indicator extraction from driver literature using Claude API.

Extracts market-observable indicators that causally predict whether a driver
is true or active.  The Claude API handles all language understanding;
this module handles input preparation, response validation, and graph-ready
normalization.

Where It Is Used:
- core.research_driver.indicator_graph_apply
- tests.test_indicator_graph_apply

- DriverIndicatorExtractionTool

- batch_extract_indicator_candidates

    normalize_relationship_judgment,
    relationship_judgment_from_direction,
)

) -> str:

        f"Driver title: {driver_title}\n"
        f"Driver search query: {driver_query}\n\n"
        f"Literature titles:\n{source_lines}\n\n"

class DriverIndicatorExtractionTool:
    """Indicator extraction tool with direct Claude API for single calls
    and BaseLLMTool-compatible interface for batch processing."""

    response_model = _IndicatorExtractionOutput

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self.system_prompt = _load_system_prompt()
        self._model = resolve_task_model("FS3.indicator_extraction")

        from packages.inference.llm.runtime import LLMRuntime

            policy=LLMPolicy(
                role="tool",
                temperature=0.0,
                max_output_tokens=1200,
                enable_cache=True,
            ),
        )

    @property

        return self._runtime.model

    @property

        return int(self._runtime.max_output_tokens)

```
"""
LLM tool for proposing sub-question targets when promoting a driver.

Given a driver's context and its parent question, proposes a structured
set of MECE outcome options suitable for a standalone sub-question.

Where It Is Used:
- services.api.routes.driver_promotion

Key Symbols:
- DriverPromotionProposalTool
- propose_driver_promotion
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import logging
from typing import Any

from pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict, Field

from packages.inference.llm import BaseLLMTool, LLMConfig
from packages.inference.llm.tiers import resolve_role_model
from packages.inference.prompts import default_prompt_store

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


def _load_system_prompt() -> str:
    return default_prompt_store().load("FS8/driver_promotion/system.md")


class _ProposedOption(BaseModel):
    model_config = ConfigDict(extra="ignore")

    label: str = Field(description="Outcome option label")
    description: str = Field(default="", description="What this outcome means")
    suggested_baseline_p: float | None = Field(
        default=None,
        description="Suggested prior probability [0.01, 0.99]",
    )


class _DriverPromotionLLMOutput(BaseModel):
    model_config = ConfigDict(extra="ignore")

    question_label: str = Field(description="Proposed question text")
    classification: str = Field(
        description="One of: binary, categorical, numeric_bracket",
    )
    options: list[_ProposedOption] = Field(
        description="2-7 MECE outcome options",
    )
    rationale: str = Field(default="", description="Why this decomposition makes sense")


class DriverPromotionProposalTool(BaseLLMTool[dict[str, Any]]):
    response_model = _DriverPromotionLLMOutput

    def __init__(self, *, model: str | None = None) -> None:
        super().__init__(
            system_prompt=_load_system_prompt(),
            temperature=0.3,
            max_output_tokens=2000,
            llm=LLMConfig(model=model or resolve_role_model("tool"), task_id="FS8.driver_promotion"),
            cache_enabled=True,
        )

    def build_messages(self, **kwargs: Any) -> list[dict[str, str]]:
        driver_label = kwargs.get("driver_label", "")
        driver_description = kwargs.get("driver_description", "")
        driver_mechanism = kwargs.get("driver_mechanism", "")
        parent_question_label = kwargs.get("parent_question_label", "")
        parent_options = kwargs.get("parent_options", [])
        parent_classification = kwargs.get("parent_classification", "")
        parent_domain = kwargs.get("parent_domain", "")
        parent_closing_date = kwargs.get("parent_closing_date", "")
        edge_judgments = kwargs.get("edge_judgments", [])

        parts = [
            f"PARENT QUESTION: {parent_question_label}",
            f"Parent classification: {parent_classification}",
            f"Parent options: {', '.join(str(o) for o in parent_options)}",
            f"Domain: {parent_domain}",
            f"Closing date: {parent_closing_date}",
```

from core.text_utils import clean_text
from packages.domain.research_driver.stage_outcomes import build_stage_outcome
from packages.inference.llm.tiers import resolve_task_model
from packages.inference.prompts import default_prompt_store

from .relationship_judgment import (
    ALLOWED_RELATIONSHIP_JUDGMENTS,
    normalize_relationship_judgment,
    relationship_judgment_from_direction,
)

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

# Compatibility alias for legacy importers and regression tests.
INDICATOR_EXTRACTION_MODEL = resolve_task_model("FS3.indicator_extraction")

def _load_system_prompt() -> str:
    return default_prompt_store().load("FS3/indicator_extraction/system.md")


def _build_user_prompt(
    *,
    driver_title: str,
    driver_query: str,
    source_lines: str,
    max_indicators: int,
---
class DriverIndicatorExtractionTool:
    """Indicator extraction tool with direct Claude API for single calls
    and BaseLLMTool-compatible interface for batch processing."""

    response_model = _IndicatorExtractionOutput

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self.system_prompt = _load_system_prompt()
        self._model = resolve_task_model("FS3.indicator_extraction")
        from packages.inference.llm import LLMConfig, LLMPolicy
        from packages.inference.llm.runtime import LLMRuntime

        self._runtime = LLMRuntime(
            llm=LLMConfig(model=self._model, task_id="FS3.indicator_extraction"),
            policy=LLMPolicy(
                role="tool",
                temperature=0.0,
                max_output_tokens=1200,
                enable_cache=True,
            ),
        )

    @property
    def model(self) -> str:
        return self._runtime.model

Perfect! Now I have all the information I need. Let me create my final report.

## Summary

I found **2 files** with inlined LLM system prompts that should be moved to the prompt store:

### 1. **services/worker/agents/ResearchDriver/insight_agent/chunk_map.py**

**Location:** Line 550-561
**Variable Name:** `_RELEVANCE_FILTER_SYSTEM`
**Prompt Content (First Line):** "You are a fast relevance filter for a forecasting system. Given a question and a document excerpt, decide whether the document contains ANY information useful for building a causal model to forecast the question."

**Details:** This is a chunk relevance filter used by the `ChunkRelevanceFilter` class (line 571-605) for Haiku-based relevance gating before expensive driver extraction.

**Corresponding .md file:** No corresponding file exists in `/prompts/FS3/`. The directory `/prompts/FS3/` contains subdirectories for `chunk_map`, `indicator_extraction`, `insight_native`, and `reduce`, but no `chunk_relevance_filter` directory.

**Location in code:** Used at line 578 in the `ChunkRelevanceFilter.__init__()` method.

---

### 2. **core/pipelines/question_set_evaluation.py**

**Location:** Line 257-280
**Variable Name:** `system_prompt` (inline in `_AssessmentTool.__init__()`)
**Prompt Content (First Line):** "You assess causal relationships between sub-question outcomes and parent question options in a forecasting graph."

**Details:** This is an inline system prompt for the nested `_AssessmentTool` class (line 252) used for LLM-assessing CONDITIONAL edges from companion drivers to parent targets in a question hierarchy.

**Corresponding .md file:** No corresponding file exists. The prompt describes the task of assessing causal relationships in hierarchy edges but does not have a canonical prompt file in the `/prompts/` directory (which has no `CrossFS` or `hierarchy_edge_assessment` subdirectory).

**Location in code:** Used within the `_AssessmentTool` class constructor, which is an inner class of the `assess_hierarchy_edges()` function.

---

## Recommendations

Both prompts should be:
1. **Extracted** to corresponding `.md` files in the prompt store
2. **Loaded** using `default_prompt_store().load()` instead of inline string constants
3. **Organized** into appropriate namespace directories (e.g., `FS3/chunk_relevance_filter/system.md` and `CrossFS/hierarchy_edge_assessment/system.md` or similar)

This follows the established pattern already used in other parts of the codebase (e.g., `FS3/chunk_map/system.md`, `FS2B/iw_verdict/system.md`).
```

  "content": "---\nname: Port review anti-patterns\ndescription: Code quality patterns to catch and reject during dev_sprint→dev porting reviews\ntype: feedback\n---\n\nPatterns found during SPI-232 port review that must be caught and flagged before any PR lands. All originated from an unreviewed \"dutty\" scratch commit on dev_sprint.\n\n**Why:** User was frustrated that multiple regressions made it into a PR that had already been \"reviewed\". Do the review yourself; do not delegate to a subagent.\n\n**How to apply:** On every ported file, diff against `dev` and check each of these explicitly before declaring the review done.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Inline LLM system prompts\n\nBad:\n```python\n_SYSTEM_PROMPT = \"You are a forecasting...\"\n```\n\nGood:\n```python\ndef _load_system_prompt() -> str:\n    return default_prompt_store().load(\"FS3/indicator_extraction/system.md\")\n```\n\nEvery LLM tool must load its system prompt from `prompts/` via `default_prompt_store().load(...)`. Inline string constants bypass versioning and hide prompt content from non-Python reviewers.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Defensive string wrapping with no value\n\nBad:\n```python\nstr(edge.type or \"\").strip().upper() == \"CONDITIONAL\"\n```\n\nGood:\n```python\nedge.type == \"CONDITIONAL\"\n```\n\n`None == \"CONDITIONAL\"` is already `False`. The defensive wrapping adds nothing, obscures intent, and suggests the author didn't understand the code.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Global mutation lazy import pattern\n\nBad:\n```python\ndef some_function():\n    global _cache\n    import heavy_module\n    _cache = heavy_module.Thing()\n```\n\nLazy imports that mutate module-level globals are a scratch/debugging pattern. No justification was ever given. Revert to the dev version.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Dropping resolve_role_model() fallback\n\nBad:\n```python\nmodel_text = str(model or \"\").strip()\nLLMConfig(model=model_text, task_id=\"FS8.driver_promotion\")\n```\n\nGood:\n```python\nLLMConfig(model=model or resolve_role_model(\"tool\"), task_id=\"FS8.driver_promotion\")\n```\n\nThe bad version happens to be safe only because `_resolve_model()` falls through to `task_id` when model is empty — but it breaks the dev pattern and hides intent.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Do not delegate port reviews to subagents\n\nReview every ported file yourself by diffing against `dev`. Subagent reviews have missed real issues (the `str().strip().upper()` pattern was missed even after being explicitly asked). The diff is the ground truth; read it.\n"
}

> AGENT

SPI-241 filed. Here's the list for future port reviews — things to explicitly check on every diffed file:

---

**1. Inline system prompts**
Every LLM tool must load from `default_prompt_store().load("FS.../system.md")`. A `_SYSTEM_PROMPT = "..."` constant is always wrong.

**2. Defensive `str().strip().upper()` on comparisons**
`str(x or "").strip().upper() == "CONDITIONAL"` adds nothing over `x == "CONDITIONAL"`. Flag any defensive wrapping on enum/type comparisons that has no real-world justification.

**3. Global mutation + lazy imports**
Any function that does `global _something` + `import module` inside the body is a scratch pattern. No justification = revert.

**4. Dropped model resolution fallback**
`model or resolve_role_model("tool")` is the dev pattern. `str(model or "").strip()` stripping the fallback out is a regression even if it happens to be safe.

**5. Do the diff yourself**
Don't delegate port reviews to subagents. Read the diff against `dev` for every file in the PR. Subagent reviews have missed real issues even when asked directly.

Perfect! Now I have enough information. Let me create a comprehensive architectural patterns reference document.

## Architectural Patterns Reference for Code Reviews

Based on a thorough survey of the codebase, here are the established patterns all contributors should follow:

---

### 1. LLM Tool Structure

**Pattern:** All LLM tools inherit from `BaseLLMTool[T]` and implement three core methods:
- `build_messages(**kwargs)` - constructs the message list (without system prompt)
- `get_response_model(**kwargs)` (optional) - returns a Pydantic model for structured output
- `postprocess(result, **kwargs)` - converts API response to desired return type

**Canonical examples:**
- `/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/.claude/worktrees/227/core/research_driver/iw_verdict_tool.py:41-90` (IWVerdictTool)
- `/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/.claude/worktrees/227/core/research_driver/driver_promotion_tool.py:57-107` (DriverPromotionProposalTool)
- `/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/.claude/worktrees/227/packages/inference/llm/base_llm_tool.py:31-141` (base class definition)

**What the wrong version looks like:** Direct LiteLLM calls outside of BaseLLMTool (only allowed in allowlist per test_llm_task_enforcement.py:16-19)

---

### 2. Stage Outcomes

**Pattern:** All stage results use `build_stage_outcome()` function with consistent schema:
- `status`: "ok" | "partial" | "failed" (coerced to "failed" if invalid)
- `reason_code`: short code identifier
- `message`: human-readable explanation
- `fallback_used`: boolean flag
- `quality_flags`: list of strings
- `extra`: additional context dict (merged into payload)

All top-level fields are normalized and empty values are stripped.

**Canonical location:** `/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/.claude/worktrees/227/packages/domain/research_driver/stage_outcomes.py:15-39`

**Usage example:** `/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/.claude/worktrees/227/core/research_driver/indicator_extraction.py:31` imports it

**Wrong version:** Returning raw exception strings or ad-hoc dicts without normalization through build_stage_outcome()

---

### 3. Node Data Builders

**Pattern:** All graph nodes use `build_node_data()` to construct standardized payloads with these top-level fields:
- `question_id` (required)
- `question_source` (normalized: underscores, lowercase)
- `source_tag` (alias for question_source)
- `origin` (dict: producer, stage, question_source)
- `provenance` (dict: source_kind, evidence_ids, sources)
- `detail` (dict: all other metadata)

Helper readers (`node_question_id()`, `node_origin()`, `node_provenance()`, etc.) provide backward compatibility for legacy flat payloads.

**Canonical location:** `/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/.claude/worktrees/227/core/node_data.py:120-155` (build_node_data) and readers at lines 158-266

**Wrong version:** Flattening everything into node.data directly; mixing legacy and new schemas without using helpers

---

### 4. Storage / DB Access

**Pattern:** Repository pattern with Protocol-based interfaces. Storage abstractions live in `packages/storage/` and implement well-defined Protocols:
- `QuestionRepository` - questions and question_sets
- `GraphStateRepository` - graphs, nodes, edges
- `GraphStoreRepository` - composite of state + question repos
- `RunStateRepository` - run execution state
- `ArtifactRepository` - file I/O
- `HistoricalReferenceRepository` - time-series and archive data

**Canonical location:** `/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/.claude/worktrees/227/packages/storage/interfaces.py:17-177`

Direct SQL access (SQLAlchemy) is confined to `/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/.claude/worktrees/227/packages/storage/database.py`

**Wrong version:** Direct database calls in domain code; bypassing the repository layer

---

### 5. Error Handling in LLM Tools

**Pattern:** Graceful degradation with validation layers:
1. Pydantic model validation wraps response (catches and logs exceptions)
2. Returns empty/default values on validation failure (never raises to caller)
3. Fallback policies decide when to inject free_knowledge/pack_fallback drivers after grounded extraction fails
4. Stage outcomes with `fallback_used=True` flag failures transparently

**Key locations:**
- Response validation: `/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/.claude/worktrees/227/core/research_driver/indicator_extraction.py:246-251` (try/except blocks return empty list)
- Fallback decision gate: `/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/.claude/worktrees/227/core/research/fallback_policy.py:69-100`
- `postprocess()` method defensive pattern: `/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/.claude/worktrees/227/core/research_driver/iw_verdict_tool.py:83-90` (returns sensible default on failure)

**Wrong version:** Raising exceptions from within postprocess(); not flagging fallback usage; crashing on validation errors

---

### 6. Imports Pattern

**Pattern:** Import hierarchy is strict:
- **Always import from** `packages.inference.llm` **directly** for public APIs: `BaseLLMTool`, `LLMConfig`, `LLMRuntime`, `resolve_role_model`, `resolve_task_model`
- **Task-specific model resolution:** Use `resolve_task_model("TASK.ID")` to look up a tier in the task config, not raw tier names
- **Role-based fallback:** Use `resolve_role_model("tool"|"agent")` for default models, not hardcoding tier names
- **Tiers imports:** Import from `packages.inference.llm.tiers` only when you need tier constants (`TIER_SMALL`, `TIER_MEDIUM`, `TIER_LARGE`) or task tier lookup

**Canonical pattern:**
```python
# Correct:
from packages.inference.llm import BaseLLMTool, LLMConfig
from packages.inference.llm.tiers import resolve_task_model

model = resolve_task_model("FS3.indicator_extraction")  # Task-aware
role_model = resolve_role_model("tool")  # Role-aware fallback

# Wrong:
from packages.inference.llm.config import LLMConfig  # Import from submodule
model = "claude-opus"  # Hardcoded model name
```

**Test enforcement:** `/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/.claude/worktrees/227/tests/test_llm_task_enforcement.py:32-48` enforces all LLMConfig calls have task_id

---

### 7. Test Structure

**Pattern:**
- `conftest.py` provides fixtures scoped globally (graph_store_repo, storage adapters)
- Test files mirror source structure
- Monkeypatching BaseLLMTool.run is the standard way to test tool orchestration without API calls
- Pydantic model validation tests are unit-level (no mocking needed)

**Canonical location:** `/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/.claude/worktrees/227/tests/conftest.py:12-21` (fixture setup)

**Example test:** `/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/.claude/worktrees/227/tests/test_question_drafter.py:56-69` (stub pattern)

**Wrong version:** Calling actual LLM APIs in unit tests; not using conftest fixtures; writing tests without monkeypatching

---

### 8. `resolve_task_model` vs `resolve_role_model`

**`resolve_task_model(task_id, ...)`** — Use when:
- You have a **named, declared task** in `config/llm_tasks.toml` (e.g., `"FS3.indicator_extraction"`)
- Task definition specifies exact tier (small/medium/large)
- You want **task-aware telemetry and policy enforcement**
- Example: `/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/.claude/worktrees/227/core/research_driver/indicator_extraction.py:44`

**`resolve_role_model(role, ...)`** — Use when:
- You have a **role** (either `"tool"` or `"agent"`)
- No specific task mapping exists (generic utility code)
- You want role-based tier defaults: `tool` → SMALL, `agent` → MEDIUM
- Example: `/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/.claude/worktrees/227/core/research_driver/driver_promotion_tool.py:65` (resolve_role_model("tool"))

**Tiers mapping:** `/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/.claude/worktrees/227/packages/inference/llm/tiers.py:38-41`

**Enforcement:** All `LLMConfig()` calls must pass `task_id=` (test_llm_task_enforcement.py enforces this)

---

### Summary of Enforcement Points

| Pattern | Enforced By | Severity |
|---------|-------------|----------|
| LLMConfig has task_id | test_llm_task_enforcement.py:32-48 | HARD |
| No direct LiteLLM calls outside allowlist | test_llm_task_enforcement.py:51-65 | HARD |
| No legacy TIER_REASONING tokens | test_llm_task_enforcement.py:68-79 | HARD |
| Stage outcomes use build_stage_outcome | Code review (not automated) | SOFT |
| Node data uses build_node_data | Code review | SOFT |
| Storage uses repository pattern | Code review | SOFT |
| Error handling has postprocess fallback | Code review | SOFT |
```

1	---
2	name: Port review anti-patterns
3	description: Code quality patterns to catch and reject during dev_sprint→dev porting reviews
4	type: feedback
5	---
6	
7	Patterns found during SPI-232 port review that must be caught and flagged before any PR lands. All originated from an unreviewed "dutty" scratch commit on dev_sprint.
8	
9	**Why:** User was frustrated that multiple regressions made it into a PR that had already been "reviewed". Do the review yourself; do not delegate to a subagent.
10	
11	**How to apply:** On every ported file, diff against `dev` and check each of these explicitly before declaring the review done.
12	
13	---
14	
15	## 1. Inline LLM system prompts
16	
17	Bad:
18	```python
19	_SYSTEM_PROMPT = "You are a forecasting..."
20	```
21	
22	Good:
23	```python
24	def _load_system_prompt() -> str:
25	    return default_prompt_store().load("FS3/indicator_extraction/system.md")
26	```
27	
28	Every LLM tool must load its system prompt from `prompts/` via `default_prompt_store().load(...)`. Inline string constants bypass versioning and hide prompt content from non-Python reviewers.
29	
30	---
31	
32	## 2. Defensive string wrapping with no value
33	
34	Bad:
35	```python
36	str(edge.type or "").strip().upper() == "CONDITIONAL"
37	```
38	
39	Good:
40	```python
41	edge.type == "CONDITIONAL"
42	```
43	
44	`None == "CONDITIONAL"` is already `False`. The defensive wrapping adds nothing, obscures intent, and suggests the author didn't understand the code.
45	
46	---
47	
48	## 3. Global mutation lazy import pattern
49	
50	Bad:
51	```python
52	def some_function():
53	    global _cache
54	    import heavy_module
55	    _cache = heavy_module.Thing()
56	```
57	
58	Lazy imports that mutate module-level globals are a scratch/debugging pattern. No justification was ever given. Revert to the dev version.
59	
60	---
61	
62	## 4. Dropping resolve_role_model() fallback
63	
64	Bad:
65	```python
66	model_text = str(model or "").strip()
67	LLMConfig(model=model_text, task_id="FS8.driver_promotion")
68	```
69	
70	Good:
71	```python
72	LLMConfig(model=model or resolve_role_model("tool"), task_id="FS8.driver_promotion")
73	```
74	
75	The bad version happens to be safe only because `_resolve_model()` falls through to `task_id` when model is empty — but it breaks the dev pattern and hides intent.
76	
77	---
78	
79	## 5. Do not delegate port reviews to subagents
80	
81	Review every ported file yourself by diffing against `dev`. Subagent reviews have missed real issues (the `str().strip().upper()` pattern was missed even after being explicitly asked). The diff is the ground truth; read it.
82	
```

{
  "file_path": "/Users/sumeet/.claude/projects/-Users-sumeet-matrix-elijah/memory/project_architectural_patterns.md",
  "content": "---\nname: Architectural patterns\ndescription: Established codebase patterns every contributor must follow — LLM tools, storage, stage outcomes, node data, imports, tests\ntype: project\n---\n\nCompiled from codebase survey during SPI-232 port review. These are enforced patterns, not preferences.\n\n**How to apply:** When writing or reviewing code, check each relevant section below before declaring it done.\n\n---\n\n## LLM tools\n\nAll LLM tools inherit from `BaseLLMTool[T]` (`packages/inference/llm/base_llm_tool.py`) and implement:\n- `build_messages(**kwargs)` — constructs user messages only (no system prompt here)\n- `get_response_model(**kwargs)` — returns a Pydantic model for structured output (optional)\n- `postprocess(result, **kwargs)` — converts API response to the tool's return type\n\nSystem prompt loaded via `_load_system_prompt()` / `default_prompt_store().load(...)`.\n\nDirect litellm calls outside `BaseLLMTool` are only allowed for the explicit allowlist in `tests/test_llm_task_enforcement.py`.\n\nEvery `LLMConfig()` call must include `task_id=`. This is enforced by `test_llm_task_enforcement.py`.\n\n---\n\n## Prompt store\n\nAll system prompts live in `prompts/` as `.md` files and are loaded via:\n```python\ndef _load_system_prompt() -> str:\n    return default_prompt_store().load(\"FS3/indicator_extraction/system.md\")\n```\n\nInline string constants (`_SYSTEM_PROMPT = \"...\"`) are wrong. See `feedback_port_review_antipatterns.md`.\n\n---\n\n## Model resolution\n\n- `resolve_task_model(\"FS3.indicator_extraction\")` — use when there is a named task declared in `config/llm_tasks.toml`. Gives task-aware telemetry.\n- `resolve_role_model(\"tool\")` / `resolve_role_model(\"agent\")` — use as a fallback when no specific task mapping exists.\n- Never hardcode model tier names or model strings.\n\n---\n\n## Storage — everything in the database\n\nAll persistent state lives in the database via the repository layer (`packages/storage/`). JSON files as storage are a retired pattern and must not be reintroduced.\n\nRepository interfaces (`packages/storage/interfaces.py`):\n- `QuestionRepository` — questions and question sets\n- `GraphStateRepository` — graphs, nodes, edges\n- `ArtifactRepository` — file/blob artifacts\n- `RunStateRepository` — run execution state\n- `HistoricalReferenceRepository` — time-series and archive data\n\nDirect SQL/SQLAlchemy access is confined to `packages/storage/database.py`. Domain code never touches SQL directly.\n\n---\n\n## Stage outcomes\n\nAll stage results use `build_stage_outcome()` (`packages/domain/research_driver/stage_outcomes.py`):\n```python\nbuild_stage_outcome(\n    status=\"ok\" | \"partial\" | \"failed\",\n    reason_code=\"short_code\",\n    message=\"human-readable explanation\",\n    fallback_used=False,\n    quality_flags=[],\n    extra={},\n)\n```\n\nNever return ad-hoc dicts or raw exception strings as stage results.\n\n---\n\n## Node data\n\nAll graph nodes are constructed with `build_node_data()` (`core/node_data.py`):\n```python\nbuild_node_data(\n    question_id=question_id,\n    question_source=question_source,\n    origin=build_origin(producer=\"...\", stage=\"...\", question_source=...),\n    provenance=build_provenance(source_kind=\"...\", sources=[...]),\n    detail={...},\n)\n```\n\nUse the helper readers (`node_question_id()`, `node_provenance()`, etc.) for reading node payloads — they handle legacy flat-payload compatibility.\n\n---\n\n## Error handling in LLM tools\n\n- `postprocess()` must never raise to the caller — return a sensible default on failure\n- Pydantic model validation wraps all LLM responses; catch and log, return empty/default on failure\n- Flag fallback usage via `fallback_used=True` in stage outcomes and `review_required=True` in payloads\n- Fallback policies live in `core/research/fallback_policy.py`\n\n---\n\n## Tests\n\n- Fixtures are in `tests/conftest.py` — use them, don't re-create storage/graph setup inline\n- LLM tools are tested by monkeypatching `BaseLLMTool.run` — never call real APIs in unit tests\n- Test files mirror source structure\n- Every new behavior needs a test (see `feedback_tests_required.md`)\n"
}
```

```markdown
## Code quality patterns

### Anti-patterns — reject on sight

- **Inline system prompts** — prompts must live in `prompts/` and load via `default_prompt_store().load(...)`. Never `_SYSTEM_PROMPT = "..."`.
- **JSON files as storage** — all persistent state goes through the database via `packages/storage/`. JSON file storage is retired.
- **Defensive string wrapping on comparisons** — `str(x or "").strip().upper() == "CONDITIONAL"` adds nothing over `x == "CONDITIONAL"`. Reject it.
- **Global mutation + lazy imports** — `global _x` inside a function body with an inline import is a scratch pattern. Revert it.
- **Dropped model resolution fallback** — use `model or resolve_role_model("tool")`, not `str(model or "").strip()`.

### Architectural patterns — always follow

- LLM tools inherit from `BaseLLMTool` and every `LLMConfig()` must have `task_id=`.
- Use `resolve_task_model("FS3.task")` for named tasks, `resolve_role_model("tool"|"agent")` for generic fallbacks. Never hardcode model names.
- All stage results use `build_stage_outcome()`. Never return ad-hoc dicts.
- All graph nodes use `build_node_data()` / `build_origin()` / `build_provenance()`.
- Direct SQL access is confined to `packages/storage/database.py`. Domain code never touches SQL.
```
